# Lootpath - the central brain

This document is the single source of truth for what Lootpath is, how it works, every decision made and why, what is verified and what is not, and where the work stands. **Every session updates this file before it ends, after anything is learned, discussed, decided, created or worked on** - not only when a PR changes code. A conversation that settles a question, refutes a premise, or moves the plan is recorded here in the same session, through a docs-only PR if nothing else is in flight. If this file and the code disagree, say so in the PR and fix whichever is wrong; never leave them disagreeing. **A new session reads §0 first**, then the sections §0 points at.

Last updated: 2026-09-11, evening (**the next lane is designed: Lootpath Roads**, `docs/ROADS-UX.md` and `docs/roads-mockups.html`. Three surfaces over one road model - In place (tooltip lines and a bag glow), Roads as the Upgrade Map slot's expanded row in three groups, This Week gated on a named night - reached through eight rounds against a devil's-advocate agent and one engineering pass against the 12.1 annotations. **Two owner decisions recorded in §7:** player-facing text names no source at all (no "QE Live", no "his", no address) and says what to do; and the 2026-09-05 "tooltips descoped" decision is reopened. Nothing is built yet; the first issue is a measurement spike on the tooltip hook. See §0, §7, §11.)

Before that: 2026-09-10, morning (**C-8 built, PR open, not merged**: the companion no longer fills QE Live's 30-item Top Gear in page order. **Two halves of 558's premise were refuted in his source before a line was written** - `SimCImportEngine.ts:712` makes a VAULT item active at import, and `Item.ts:174` gives a Catalyst clone its source's active flag - so the vault was never outside the 30 and there is no such thing as an active bag item to deselect. What the cap really cost was the clones and most of the bags. The driver now reads every card once, keeps everything active, then spends the room on vault items, then every clone, then bag items a slot at a time; the leftovers are named in `Data/QEVerdict.lua` and printed on the Equip Now and Vault tabs. **Not measured: a real fork run** - the owner's watcher owns that browser profile (§9). The cap decision is still his. See §0, §7, §9, §11.)

## 0. Where we are (read this first; keep it current)

**As of 2026-09-09, night (`main` at 074e511 after PR #59; every `ai-ready` issue 514-548 and 555 is Done; the M5 lane 549-554 is filed, 551 M5-2 is merged, 550 M5-1 is merged, and 552 M5-3 is built with its PR open).** Everything below is a pointer; the detail is in the numbered sections.

**Merged to `main`:** PR #1 this document; PR #2 WKE-514 skeleton, five CI gates, captures `env`/`inventory`/`vault`; PR #3 WKE-515 first in-client transcript, branch protection; PR #4 WKE-516 Inventory module and `ns.ParseItemLink`; PR #5 WKE-518 QEImport parser (its real-fixture test is `pending` until 519); PR #6 WKE-527 QE Live fork branch and the finding that the live site has no Download JSON control; PR #7 WKE-522 part 1, the Journal adapter, async captures and `/lootpath capture journal` (merged 2026-09-06 20:56 UTC); PR #8 this section and the one-worktree-per-issue rule; PR #9 the pre-Tuesday plan (docs); PR #10 WKE-523 first visit, the 2026-09-06 journal transcript and before-reset vault pair; PR #11 WKE-519 the first genuine QE Live export; PR #12 WKE-522 part 2, `ns.Journal` and the walk's second read (merged 22:16 UTC); PR #13 the brain sync and the client re-sync; **PR #14 WKE-520, `ns.Match`, the paste editbox, the Equip Now panel and the Settings page**; PR #16 `tools\check.ps1` failing its gate when StyLua or LuaLS is missing instead of passing it; **PR #15 WKE-524 (M3-3), `ns.Vault`, the Upgrade Map and Vault panels, and their tabs in the window**; **PR #21 WKE-530 (M3-5), the five UI findings from the first in-game run**; PR #24 WKE-531 (S-1), the fork driven headless.

**Merged since:** PR #24 (S-1, WKE-531): the headless fork driver, the `/simc` fixture and the two Upgrade Finder fixtures. PR #26 (S-2, WKE-532): the SimC profile builder, its SavedVariables reader, 32 Node guards, and four new reads in `capture env` (`UnitLevel`, `UnitRace`, `GetCurrentRegionName`, `GetCurrentRegion`). PR #25 (C-2, WKE-534): the addon half - `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua` (a committed placeholder that sets nothing) in the `.toc` after `Core.lua`, `ns.Companion` validating and importing it at load through `ns.QEImport.Parse`, per-content-type storage with the later of file and paste winning, the source on the window's verdict line and in `/lootpath status`, `/lootpath refresh` (out of combat only), and `tools\sync.ps1` keeping the companion's file unless `-IncludeData`. **PR #27 (C-1, WKE-533): the companion itself** - `tools/companion/`, which lifts S-1's fork driver and S-2's profile builder into one program and writes C-2's contract. PR #31 (C-3, WKE-536): `/lootpath refresh` takes the `env`, `inventory` and `vault` captures before it reloads. **PR #28 (M3-6, WKE-535): the Upgrade Finder import** - `Modules/UFImport.lua`, the paste box routing on schema, `ns.Companion` handing `qe-live-upgradefinder` documents to it instead of refusing them, and the Upgrade Map's second path to a number (itemID + item level, exact or nothing).

**Merged 2026-09-09:** PR #45 (WKE-545, M3-10, the cross-level join) and PR #46 (WKE-540, C-6, the named scenarios).

**Open:** nothing but this note. **The Roads lane is filed (2026-09-12):** WKE-561 R-0 spike, 562 R-1 road model and plan sentence, 563 R-2 In place (blocked by 561, 562), 564 R-3 Roads row and the Vault plan headline (blocked by 562), 565 R-4 Delves and Crafted rows (blocked by 562), 566 R-5 This Week (blocked by 562; built only after a week with R-2 and R-3), 567 C-10 excluded list with item IDs (independent), 568 R-H the owner's three answers (bag addon, vendor-window capture, the sentences' voice). Still owed by the owner: 557 (vault `OnUIInteract`), 558's cap question, 549, 554, 568. The owner runs the companion watcher in his own window; no session starts one. Fork and Docker Desktop are up.

**Issues:** done 514-520, 522-524, 527, 530-545 (every `ai-ready` issue so far); 546 M3-11 merged 2026-09-09 (PR #51; the one thing still owed is a transcript carrying the by-ID probe, §11); **547 M3-12 built 2026-09-09, PR open, not merged** (a vault reward the client has not loaded yet is said in words, the client is asked for it, the tab redraws when it answers; still owed: the in-client proof - the owner's next fresh login with the Vault tab open, §11); waiting on the owner 521 (two leftovers: the buttons greying in combat, Equip from the bank), 525 (the week of real use, started 2026-09-08 - findings become issues the way 541-546 did), 528 (the QE Live PR to Voulk, and the gate for anyone but the owner using the companion); 526 (v0.1.0) after 525; 529 gated on 528. **Roads lane (2026-09-12):** 561 R-0, 562 R-1, 563 R-2, 564 R-3, 565 R-4, 566 R-5, 567 C-10, 568 R-H (owner).

**What the product does today (all merged and in the owner's client):**
- **The loop:** `/lootpath refresh` takes the `env`, `inventory`, `vault` and `currencies` snapshots and reloads; the companion (`tools/companion`, `node companion.js --watch`) reads the SavedVariables, builds the SimC profile, drives the owner's local QE Live fork headless and writes `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua`; a second `/lootpath refresh` loads it. No `/simc`, no browser, no paste. A refresh with unchanged gear costs nothing (C-4). Twelve Top Gear documents come back per run - `asOffered` / `catalyzed` / `maxed` x Dungeon / Raid (C-6) - plus the Upgrade Finder at +2, +4, +6, +8, +10 and Raid (C-7); all three QE Live import boxes are set explicitly, both upgrade boxes off by default (C-5).
- **Equip Now:** QE Live's `asOffered` top set joined to the scan by exact item key; Equip and Equip all work in game (521); a vault option in his best set is a pointer to the Vault tab, not a "not owned" swap (541). **Since 550 (M5-1, PR open) a row is an item line** - the item's own icon, a quality-tinted `WhiteIconFrame` border, the item level in the icon's corner, the name in quality colour, a grey second line and a status badge - a swap is drawn as worn icon → arrow → best icon with the Equip button, the five counts are chips above the list, and the list scrolls.
- **Upgrade Map:** by slot (every drop by item level, his `upgradePercent` where a key-level document carries the item at the client's level - the cross-level join, 545) and by run (best upgrade / most upgrades, never probability-weighted - 542); dungeon runs rank since 545. **Since 552 (M5-3, PR open) both views are a `WowScrollBoxList` over a data provider**: by slot is a collapsible section per slot, headed by the icon and level of what that slot wears, over M5-1 item lines whose second line is `boss - instance, difficulty`, with `[owned 305]` as the Owned tag and his verdict as the badge with `(at +6)` in grey after it; by run is a card per run - instance art, name, difficulty, `best +1.83%` as the badge, the drop count in grey - that opens onto its rated drops. One difficulty dropdown replaces the row of buttons. Which sections are shut and which cards are open is remembered per character.

- **Vault:** the real reward shape (538); one line per scenario under each gear option (540, and since 548 there are **four** scenarios, `asOffered` / `catalyzed` / `thisWeek` / `maxed`, with `thisWeek` the default highlight); a headline block leading with his pick under the owner's chosen scenario, "none - nothing beats your set; closest: …" when that is the truth, the five Mistcrest counts from the client, and the Catalyst charge (544/548), which 546 now reads by currency ID - Venomblight Manaflux 3465 - so the line says "needs a Catalyst charge (you have 1 of 8)" off the live client and keeps saying "not readable" over the 2026-09-08 transcript, which genuinely does not know. **547 (M3-12, PR open):** a reward the client has not loaded yet reads `name pending (item 275547) - level pending` - or `Preyhunter's Lantern (from the last capture) - level pending` when a stored vault snapshot names the same reward - never `[] (nil)`; the client is asked for the item and the tab redraws once when it answers, and its counts, its scenario lines and the pick are unchanged because the key was never lost. **555 (M3-14):** a **fifth** headline line under the four scenario lines - `one charge (this week, Catalyst used once): catalyze your Hide of Pestilence (302) into the tier chest - 1.73% behind` - read off the `thisWeek` document's own alternatives, and `not in QE Live's export - no set he ranked spends the charge just once` when none of his sets qualifies. It is absent entirely when the question does not arise (no `thisWeek` document, the Catalyst box off in the run that made one, or no inventory scan). **556 (M3-15, PR open) corrects what "the charge" counts:** converting a Great Vault reward spends one too, so every clone is counted and on the owner's own documents that fifth line is the absence sentence; the scenario lines gained the vault half of the catalyze sentence - `and catalyze your Hide of Pestilence (302) into the tier chest and the vault's Scavenger's Spaulders (308) into the tier shoulder` - which is said even with no bags read.
- **The Vault tab is a grid since 553 (M5-4, PR open).** Three rows of three cells - Raids, Dungeons, World, in Blizzard's order and under `RAIDS`/`DUNGEONS`/`WORLD` when the client has them - each cell the reward the client offers there drawn as an M5-1 item line, with the slot and M3-7's level wording under the name and the highlighted scenario's own line below that. A cell with no reward says Blizzard's own threshold sentence (the activity's `raidString` on a Raid row, `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_*` otherwise), falling back to the progress wording. The pick wears the Great Vault's own `evergreen-weeklyrewards-reward-selected`; when the headline says `none - nothing in the vault beats your set`, nothing glows and the closest option is labelled `closest`. Every scenario's line is in the cell's tooltip, verbatim from `Panel.ScenarioLine`, so the hover and the text panel cannot disagree. The scenario dropdown sits on the tab. Under the grid: a currency strip with the client's own icons and names, and every option Blizzard's frame does not draw (Concession, "Also receive") named rather than dropped. `Panel.Lines` did not change, so `/lootpath status` and every text test read exactly what they did.

## 1. What Lootpath is

**One sentence:** Lootpath puts QE Live's gear answers inside World of Warcraft, next to the loot they are about.

**The three promises, on one in-game frame:**
1. **Equip Now** - what you should be wearing right now, from what you own, with one-click equip.
2. **Upgrade Map** - for each gear slot, which dungeon boss or raid boss drops a candidate, and (where QE Live has ranked it) how much better it is.
3. **Vault** - which Great Vault option to take this week.

**The product rule, above every other rule:** **Lootpath never computes a healer value.** Every healing number on screen is QE Live's, transported unchanged from its export. Lootpath's own work is the scanner (gear, bags, bank, vault), the loot map (Encounter Journal walk), matching, and display. No network, no backend, no behaviour in combat, no estimate of its own to fill a gap.

**Who it is for, in order:** the owner (MVP = the owner alone, on one character); then healers generally; DPS via Raidbots/MythicSim output is a later extension of the same import interface, not an MVP goal.

**Why this exists:** the audience's whole gearing loop today is alt-tab: `/simc` in game, paste into QE Live in a browser, read the answer there, alt-tab back and try to remember it at the vault or when a drop appears. QE Live is right about the math; everything around the math happens where the game cannot see it. Mr. Mythical solves this in-game for DPS with a SimulationCraft-trained model; SimulationCraft cannot model healing, so no healer equivalent could be built that way. Lootpath is "Mr. Mythical for healers" with QE Live as the brain.

## 3. The ecosystem, stated precisely (premise-checked 2026-09-03/05)

| Tool | What it does | Relation to Lootpath |
|---|---|---|
| **QE Live** (questionablyepic.com/live, Voulk) | Healer gearing brain: Top Gear (best set from what you own + vault), Upgrade Finder (values drops you do not own, by boss), Vault, trinket analysis. Web only. | **The brain.** Lootpath is the in-game reader of its output. |
| **Mr. Mythical** (addon) | In-game equip/loot/vault/crest recommendations from a neural-net DPS model. `/mrdps`. | The shape Lootpath copies, for healers. DPS only; cannot do healers. Open-source reference for journal walking and loadout ranking (respect its licence; mirror patterns, copy nothing). |
| **Pawn** | Same-slot item comparison in tooltips from imported stat weights (QE Live and WoWAnalyzer export weights for it). | Already owns tooltip comparison. **Lootpath does not do tooltips.** |
| **GearingWise, GearWish** | Tooltip upgrade advisor; loot wishlist. | More reasons tooltips are not a gap. |
| **Healer Stat Weights** | Computed healer weights live in-game; 234K downloads; appears unmaintained. | Proof the in-game healer audience exists. |
| **Raidbots / MythicSim** | DPS sims; Droptimizer output consumed by addons. | The DPS import path later; not MVP. |
| **WowCoach.gg** | AI log coaching, $5.99-12.99/mo, no visible traction. | The rejected product shape. AI is never the pitch. |

**The gap, exactly:** not "stat comparison for healers" (Pawn), not "loot source lists" (the Adventure Guide), but **QE Live's verdicts shown in-game next to the loot map and the vault.** That combination exists nowhere.

**Economics:** side project that pays for itself at most. Free addon, tip jar later. Comparable free-plus-Patreon tools: QE Live, Mr. Mythical, wowaudit (patron count NOT public; the "3,400" figure from an early draft was unverified and is withdrawn).

## 4. QE Live: everything we know (all read from its public source, branch `dev`)

- Repo: github.com/Voulk/QuestionablyEpic. **No licence file** = all rights reserved. Actively pushed (2026-09-02). We read it for understanding and schemas; we copy no code and no data tables.
- **Top Gear exports.** `src/General/Modules/TopGear/Report/TopGearJSONExport.ts`, present since at least 2026-05-20 (last touched by "handle duplicates"). Schema string `"qe-live-droptimizer"`, `version: 1`. Payload:
  ```
  { schema, version, exportedAt (ISO), player: {name, realm, region, spec, gameType: "Retail"|"Classic"},
    contentType, reportId,
    topSet: { score (hardScore), stats (setStats), items: [Item] },
    differentials: [ { scorePercent, hpsDifference, items: [Item], gems: [int] } ] }
  Item = { slot, id, level, bonusIDs: [int], gems: [int], enchant: string, tertiary: string, setId: int, isVault: bool, isExclusive: bool, source: {} }
  ```
  Sign conventions: `scoreDifference` (exported as `scorePercent`) is a percent where **positive means the alternative is worse**; `rawDifference` (exported as `hpsDifference`) is a raw score delta where **negative means the alternative is worse**. Stated in the exporter's comment *and* confirmed by the arithmetic (§9, 2026-09-06; **corrected 2026-09-08 - the Retail path is `TopGearEngine.ts`, not `TopGearEngineShared.js`, and it DOES scale `rawDifference` by the player's HPS**). The SIGNS are unchanged by that correction. Pinned as named constants in `QEImport` and again in its spec.
  `topSet.items` are the chosen items of the best set (falls back to the full list if none flagged). `differentials` = alternative sets: only the items that differ from the top set, with the delta.
  It is a **file download** (`qe-droptimizer-<name>-<id>.json`), not a clipboard copy. **Nothing on GitHub consumes this schema** (code search 2026-09-04).
  **Refuted 2026-09-06 (WKE-527): there is no Download JSON control.** `downloadTopGearJSON` has no caller anywhere in `src`, in any of his 20 branches (`git grep` across `upstream/*`); the exporter has one commit and is dead code. The live site cannot produce the file today. The owner's fork branch `lootpath/upgrade-finder-export` wires it (Export menu: Download JSON, Copy JSON). Until that lands upstream, the owner gets an export by running the fork locally (`npm start` in `c:\Code\qe-live-fork`, import `/simc`, Top Gear, Export > Download JSON); the engine is the same code as the site. **Done once, 2026-09-06:** the control produced a well-formed `version: 1` file on the first try (§9).
  **Upgrade Finder report shape (read 2026-09-06):** the report page holds the *shortened* object `shortenReport` stores via `addUpgradeReport.php`: `{ id, playername, realm, region, spec, contentType, gameType, ufSettings, equippedItems: Item[], results[] }` where each `results` entry is `processItem`'s `{ item (id), level, dropLoc, dropType, dropDifficulty, score, rawDiff, percDiff }` plus `source {instanceId, encounterId}` and `slot` added by the page. **`percDiff` and `rawDiff` are `new - base`: positive means an upgrade**, the opposite sign to the Top Gear differentials. The fork's `qe-live-upgradefinder` v1 export names them `upgradePercent` / `hpsGain` (see `docs/qe-live-pr.md`).
  **His checks:** `.eslintignore` ends with a bare `src/`, so his ESLint config lints nothing in the source tree and has no TypeScript parser; `tsc --noEmit` on `dev` reports 205 errors; `react-scripts test` is the runnable check.
- **Upgrade Finder does NOT export.** `UpgradeFinderReport.js` has no export/copy/download path; `UpgradeItemData.js` is marked "Currently unimplemented". The in-memory result (`runUpgradeFinder`, `UpgradeFinderEngine.js` ~line 105) is `UpgradeFinderResult { itemSet, differentials, contentType, id }` where each item possibility carries `source[0].instanceId` / `encounterId` (used by `getSetItemLevel`) and differentials carry score deltas. An export mirroring Top Gear's is roughly 50 lines - in his repo.
- **Vault reaches QE Live via the SimulationCraft addon**: `simc-addon/core.lua` ~line 1294 reads `C_WeeklyRewards:HasAvailableRewards()`, `GetActivities()`, `GetItemHyperlink(rewardInfo.itemDBID)` and exports the options. QE Live marks them `vaultItem`/`isVault`.
- **Relationship history:** Voulk reviewed Healper's advice layer harshly and correctly (2026-09-01, Discord). Lootpath must read as the opposite: computes nothing, defers to his engine, drives users to his site. Outreach is the owner's (QE-2), after there is a working addon to show, leading with "your export has no consumer; here is one, plus two buttons" - a copy-to-clipboard control and the Upgrade Finder export, offered as a PR from the owner's fork. Never mention Healper unless he does.

## 5. Data flow (the round-trip)

1. In game: SimulationCraft addon `/simc` (includes equipped, bags, and vault when available) - copy.
2. Browser: run **Top Gear** (M+ by default), Export > **Download JSON**. **On the companion's path this is driven headlessly, and since C-5 the two upgrade checkboxes are set explicitly before Submit** (both off by default), so a vault option and the gear it would replace are valued at the same point on their upgrade tracks; the pair is recorded in `Data/QEVerdict.lua` as `qeSettings`. **Until the fork's PR lands upstream that control exists only on the fork**, so the owner runs it locally (`npm start` in `c:\Code\qe-live-fork`) rather than on questionablyepic.com/live.
3. In game: `/lootpath`, paste the JSON text into the editbox. **The box takes either export** (M3-6): `UI.ImportAny` reads the `schema` string out of the text and hands it to `QEImport` (Top Gear) or `UFImport` (Upgrade Finder); each validates schema, version 1 and Retail for itself and stores its verdict in SavedVariables with its `exportedAt`. A third schema is refused by name, naming both the ones Lootpath reads.
4. `Inventory` scans; `Journal` walks (cached); `Vault` reads; `Match` joins the Top Gear verdict to the inventory by item key, and the Upgrade Map joins the Upgrade Finder verdict to the journal by itemID + item level.
5. Three panels render. Equip Now can equip out of combat.

Constraints that shape it: addons have **no network** and **cannot read files** (hence paste); SavedVariables flush **only on `/reload` or logout**; secret values apply in combat/encounters/M+/PvP (spike-verified they lift out of combat; the guard stays on every read regardless); the bank is readable only while the bank frame is open.

**Since C-6 (WKE-540) step 2 is not one Top Gear run but one per named scenario, and each is its own IMPORT.** QE Live's three import checkboxes act at import (`runSimC` is handed their state at Submit), so asking "what is this vault option now" and "what is it through the Catalyst" means pasting the same profile again with different boxes. The four are fixed and named: `asOffered` (all off), `catalyzed` (catalyze on), **`thisWeek` (vault upgrade + catalyze, ALL off - M3-13)**, `maxed` (all three on). Top Gear only; the Upgrade Finder is not a scenario question and rides in whichever pass already asks for the configured `qeAutoUpgradeVault`/`qeAutoUpgradeAll` pair with catalyze off - the `asOffered` pass under the default, an import of its own otherwise. The three what-ifs are asked only when the profile carries a vault section with gear in it, or under `--force`. Each Top Gear document records its `scenario` and the three boxes that produced it; the addon files verdicts under `(content type, scenario)` and **Equip Now and the Upgrade Map read `asOffered` and nothing else**.

**Since C-7 (WKE-543) step 2 is not one Upgrade Finder run but one per Mythic+ key level.** His Upgrade Finder values every dungeon drop at the single key `ufSettings.dungeon` names, so the dungeon Upgrade Finder is run once per level in `upgradeFinderKeyLevels` (default `+2, +4, +6, +8, +10`) and each document records `keyLevel`; the addon files an Upgrade Finder verdict under `(contentType, keyLevel)` and the Upgrade Map reads the one matching the level its walk previewed.

**The companion path (decision 2026-09-07; S-1/S-2 spikes, C-1/C-2 build it):** the same round-trip with no human in the middle. (1) **`/lootpath refresh` takes the `env`, `inventory` and `vault` captures, in that order, and then reloads**, so what reaches disk is the gear the character is wearing now rather than whatever was last captured by hand (C-3, WKE-536; before it refresh only reloaded and the companion valued the newest stored snapshot, which on 2026-09-07 was two days and one spec old). A plain `/reload` still flushes, and still flushes only what was already captured. (2) A Node companion on the owner's PC (`tools/companion/`) watches that file, builds the SimC text profile from Lootpath's own records (mirroring the SimC addon's Unlicense exporter), drives the owner's local fork at localhost:3000 headless to run Top Gear and Upgrade Finder for Dungeon and Raid, and writes the four JSON documents as Lua strings into `Interface\AddOns\Lootpath\Data\QEVerdict.lua` (temp file + rename). (3) `/reload` again: the addon loads that file - it is in the `.toc`, a committed placeholder ships with the addon - and runs each document through the same `QEImport.Parse` as a paste, pins and refusals included. The paste box stays as the manual path. **Both halves are built.** C-1 (WKE-533, PR #27) is `tools/companion/`: it finds the SavedVariables by glob under `WTF\Account\*` (the account name is never written into the repo and never printed), reads them with S-2's reader and builds the profile with S-2's builder (both lifted into `tools/companion/lib/`, so there is one copy of each and not two), drives the fork through Playwright with a persistent browser profile, and writes the chunk temp-file-then-rename. **The addon half (C-2, WKE-534, PR #25):** `ns.Companion` at load, `/lootpath refresh` for both reloads, the source named on screen, and a repo sync that no longer overwrites the companion's file. The file shape it reads is §6's contract, and it is what C-1 must write. This is the Raider.IO pattern: their desktop client writes score databases into the addon folder the same way. Floor: two reloads, zero alt-tabs; nothing in the client can go lower, because SavedVariables reach disk only on reload and addon files load only on reload. **The second reload is free (C-4, WKE-537, PR #32):** every `/reload` writes SavedVariables and the watcher fires on every write, so step (3) used to start a QE Live run of its own over the same gear - 17.7 s and four byte-identical documents, measured on the owner's machine 2026-09-07 21:07. The companion now fingerprints the profile it is about to ask QE Live about (SHA-256 over the profile text with its four volatile header lines removed), remembers in `.state/last-profile.json` the fingerprint of the last verdict it actually wrote, and skips the run when the fingerprint agrees, the remembered path is the file this run would write, and that file is still on disk; it logs one line instead. `--force` runs regardless. The debounce (1500 ms) is untouched and was never the answer: two reloads twenty seconds apart really are two writes and the second must be READ, because the owner may have opened the bank and captured between them - it is the profile, not the write, that decides.

---

## 6. Architecture

One shipped package `Lootpath/` (what the packager zips) plus `spec/` (tests) and `tools/` (dev scripts). One namespace table `ns` shared via `.toc` load order; modules register on `ns`; no globals except the slash handler.

| Module | Responsibility | Interface (contract) | Testable headless? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Core.lua` (shipped M0-1) | lifecycle, events, `/lootpath` dispatcher (`capture <name>`, `capture`, `capture wipe`, `status`, `refresh`, `help`), AceDB SavedVariables, `ns.Safe(value)` secret guard, `ns.CopyRaw(table)` guarded deep copy (secret, cycle, depth 10, 50k nodes, UI objects), `ns.Probe(fn, ...)` pcall-to-positional-table, `ns.ItemKey(id, bonusIDs)`, `ns.BonusIDsFromKey(key)`, `ns.EpochFromISO(iso, now)`, `ns.RegisterCapture/RunCapture` | `ItemKey` = `"<itemID>:<sorted bonusIDs joined by :>"`, bare `"<itemID>"` with none, `nil` for garbage; ONE definition used by QEImport and Match, with `BonusIDsFromKey` (C-6) as its inverse beside it so no caller has to know the format carries colons. `RunCapture(name, onComplete, args)` refuses in combat (`{ok=false, reason="combat"}`), before the DB exists, for unknown names, and while another capture is running. A capture registered `{ async = true }` is handed `finish(data, failure)` instead of returning data, answers `{ok=true, pending=true}` at once, and is abandoned after `ns.CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (180) if it never calls back; `finish` counts only once (M3-1). `EpochFromISO` (C-2) is the ONE reader of an ISO 8601 UTC stamp - QE Live's `exportedAt` and the companion's `writtenAt` - so an age on screen and a freshness decision can never disagree about what a timestamp means; `UI.AgeSeconds` is a subtraction on top of it | yes (`spec/core_spec.lua`) |
| `Captures.lua` (shipped M0-1; `currencies` added M3-9, its by-ID probe M3-11) | `/lootpath capture env`, `inventory`, `vault`, `currencies` raw dumps | snapshot `{ name, capturedAt, capturedAtLocal, build, addonVersion, sawSecret, durationMs, data }` appended to `db.global.captures[name]`. Reads only; every client function called is named in the file (bank predicates are an allow-list) - never a namespace walk, because `C_Bank` also moves items and money. **`currencies` (M3-9, WKE-544)** walks `C_CurrencyInfo.GetCurrencyListSize()` and `GetCurrencyListInfo(i)` over every index - **headers kept**, because the client's own grouping is the evidence for which entries are the season's upgrade currencies - and adds `GetCurrencyInfo(currencyID)` for every non-header entry. All three are in Ketho's `CurrencyInfoDocumentation.lua` (`CurrencyInfo`: `name`, `description`, `currencyID`, `isHeader`, `quantity`, `maxQuantity`, `quantityEarnedThisWeek`, `iconFileID`, ...). Guard before coerce: a secret `CurrencyInfo` comes back from `ns.Safe` as a marker string and is recorded rather than indexed, and it produces no second call. **M3-11 (WKE-546) adds the by-ID half**, because the list walk is the currency TAB and the tab shows only the rows of EXPANDED headers: the capture also calls `GetCurrencyInfo(id)` for every ID in `ns.Currencies.KNOWN_IDS` (read at capture time - `Modules/` loads after this file) and stores the packs under `data.byID[id]`, with `data.probedIDs` naming what it asked. `isHeaderExpanded` keeps being recorded; it is the field that proved the list is a scroll state. `ExpandCurrencyList` would make the hidden rows appear and is **not** called | yes (`spec/captures_spec.lua`, over the stub; the `currencies` half is Blizzard-documented placeholders until the owner's transcript lands) |
| `Modules/ItemData.lua` (M5-1, WKE-550) | the ONE item loader: what the client can say about an item with no server round trip, and how a caller is told when the rest arrives | `ns.ItemData.Instant(itemInfo)` -> `{ itemID, itemType, itemSubType, equipLoc, slot, icon, classID, subclassID }` off `C_Item.GetItemInfoInstant` (static data - the icon is its FIFTH return, and `slot` is QE Live's vocabulary through `ns.Inventory.SlotForEquipLoc`); `ns.ItemData.Cached(itemInfo)` -> `{ name, link, quality, itemLevel }` or **nil while the client cannot name it**, which is Blizzard's own gate for RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO; `ns.ItemData.Watch(itemID, onCheck, onGiveUp)` is the mechanism M3-12 built inside `Vault` - ONE `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` per itemID per episode, both load events (`ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` / `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED`) and a bounded timer behind them (`WAIT_SECONDS` x `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, 8 x 0.25 s, the journal's shape) - and `ns.ItemData.Request(itemID, cb)` is the one-shot a drawn row wants: it fires at most once, when the client can name the item, and **never after `handle:Cancel()`**, which is what stops a re-used row from being filled in with the item it used to hold. A failed load ends the episode without a guess (Blizzard's AsyncCallbackSystem does the same); the bound ending leaves the row exactly as it is; nothing runs in combat and the episode is kept until it is over. `FUNCTION_NAMES` names the four C_Item calls literally | yes (`spec/itemdata_spec.lua`, 15 tests: static data before a load, one request for two rows, cancel, the bound, the timer as an occasion, a failed load, combat, and the listener stopping when nothing is waiting) |
| `Modules/Inventory.lua` (shipped M1-1) | equipped + owned bags 0..5 + bank tabs (only while `C_Bank.CanViewBank` says so) -> records | `ns.Inventory:Scan()` -> `{ ok = true, records, bankAvailable, secretsSeen }` or `{ ok = false, reason = "combat" }`; record `{ key, itemID, link, itemLevel, bonusIDs (sorted), slot, equipLoc, location = equipped/bag/bank, bag, slotIndex, name, quality, icon }`. **`slot` uses QE Live's vocabulary** (`Head`, `Neck`, `Shoulder`, `Back`, `Chest`, `Wrist`, `Hands`, `Waist`, `Legs`, `Feet`, `Finger`, `Trinket`, `1H Weapon`, `2H Weapon`, `Offhand`, `Shield`, read from `TopGearExports.ts`), not the issue's `HEAD` style, because Match groups per slot against the export. Non-gear (consumables, bags, shirts, profession tools, keystones) yields no record. `itemLevel` = `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo(link)[1]`; quality falls back to `ContainerItemInfo.quality` when `GetItemInfo` is uncached. **`icon` (M5-1) is the FIFTH return of `GetItemInfoInstant`** - static data, so it answers for an item whose full data has not arrived - with `ContainerItemInfo.iconFileID` as the fallback, exactly as quality has one. Every read passes `ns.Safe`; a secret is dropped and counted. `ns.ParseItemLink` (Core) is the one link parser | yes: `spec/inventory_spec.lua` replays the transcript through the stub and compares to golden fixtures under `spec/fixtures/expected/` |
| `Modules/Journal.lua` (M3-1, both PRs shipped) | `ns.JournalAdapter` (every EJ call, raw, secret-guarded, plus the async two-stage walk and `capture journal`) + `ns.Journal` (pure aggregator) | Every adapter entry point hands back `ns.Probe` packs unchanged (`{n=..., ...}`, `{absent=true}`, `{error=...}`) with each value through `ns.CopyRaw`; secrets are masked and counted in `Adapter.secretsSeen`. `Adapter.FUNCTION_NAMES` is the literal list of every client function the file calls (28 as of PR 2), and `Availability()` reports which of them this client lacks. `Walk(opts, onDone)` selects instance / difficulty / loot filter / preview level per target, waits for the loot LIST (`LOOT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` x `LOOT_WAIT_SECONDS`, 8 x 0.25s), reads it, and **then reads it again** if any row's ITEM data is missing (`ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS` x `ITEM_DATA_WAIT_SECONDS`, 8 x 0.25s) - `record.loot` is the first read, `record.reread` the second, both kept so a capture shows what the second filled. `Adapter.RowIsPending(row)` is Blizzard's own gate (`itemInfo.name` missing = RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO); `Adapter.EncounterNames(read)` resolves each row's boss with `EJ_GetEncounterInfo`, as Blizzard's loot button does. **`ns.Journal:Build(opts)` -> `sources, summary, fromCache`**, `sources` being the contract map `{ [itemID] = { {instanceID, instanceName, encounterID, encounterName, difficultyID, itemLevel, slot, isRaid, pending, itemKey, name} ... } }` (`itemKey` and `name` added by M3-3; `summary.previewMythicPlusLevel` with them), cached in `db.global.journalCache` keyed `(build, seasonID, specID, difficultyIDs)` - e.g. `69587|18|105|2:8:15:16:23` - and every entry from another build dropped on the way in. `slot` is QE Live's vocabulary, taken from the row's `GetItemInfoInstant` equipLoc through **`ns.Inventory.SlotForEquipLoc`**, not from `EncounterJournalItemInfo.slot` (that field is the client's localised UI text). A row whose item data had not arrived is carried with `pending = true` and a nil `itemLevel` and `slot`: **`pending` is "not known yet", never "no item level"**. **`itemLevel` is not a field of `EncounterJournalItemInfo`** (Blizzard docs, 2026-09-05): it comes from `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` on the loot row's `link`, as the SimC addon does for vault links. Build is pure Lua over a walk result, so like `QEImport.Parse` it carries no combat guard; the walk in front of it does | aggregator yes, over the committed transcript (`spec/journal_spec.lua`: 613 rows -> 558 sources over 375 items, slot vocabulary, a raid item and a dungeon item, the cache key, the second read, plus a golden under `spec/fixtures/expected/journal-20260906-161213.lua`); adapter only via captures, with the stub proving the behaviour around the client - probing, missing functions, secret masking, both waits, both bounds, combat, restore |
| `Modules/Vault.lua` (shipped M3-3; pending rewards and the second read M3-12) | `C_WeeklyRewards` adapter: the week's options, their progress, and the item behind each generated reward - and, since M3-12, the request and the second read for a reward the client has not loaded yet | `ns.Vault.Options()` -> `{ ok = true, options, hasAvailableRewards, canClaimRewards, secondsUntilWeeklyReset, secretsSeen }` or `{ ok = false, reason = "combat"\|"no vault API" }`; option `{ type, typeLabel, index, id, threshold, progress, level, activityTierID, raidString, unlocked, rewards }` (`raidString` added M5-4: the client's own threshold sentence for a Raid row, recorded verbatim - `%d` and plural marker and all - because Blizzard's own vault frame prefers it over `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_RAID` and the drawn grid has to say the same thing; nothing formats it here); reward `{ itemDBID, itemID, key, link, itemLevel, slot, equipLoc, name, quality, quantity, icon }` (`icon` added M5-1, from the same fifth return, so a pending row still draws its own icon). The same four calls the SimulationCraft addon makes, all named in `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES`, never discovered by walking `C_WeeklyRewards` (it also carries `ClaimReward`). **Guard before coerce**: `tonumber()` of a secret is `nil`, so every field passes `ns.Safe` first and is coerced after, or the secret would be swallowed uncounted. A currency or quest reward (`Enum.CachedRewardType`) is skipped rather than shown as an item with no level; a reward whose link never arrives keeps its row and carries no key rather than a wrong one. **M3-12 (WKE-547): a reward whose link ARRIVES with an empty name is `pending`** - measured 2026-09-09 08:59, right after a client restart, on four of five rewards: `|h[]|h`, `GetItemInfo` and `GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` nil, `GetItemInfoInstant` still answering the slot, itemID / bonus IDs / key intact. `Vault.RewardIsPending` is Blizzard's own gate (`itemInfo.name` missing = RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO); a pending record carries `name = nil`, `itemLevel = nil`, its slot when the client's static data gives one, and its key. `Options(opts)` stays synchronous and returns the pending records; unless `opts.request == false` it calls `Vault.RequestItemData` on each, which asks `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID(itemID)` once per itemID per episode, registers `ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` and `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED` on the module's own frame, and arms a timer (`ITEM_DATA_WAIT_SECONDS` x `ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, 8 x 0.25 s, the journal's bound). `Vault.OnItemData` (out of combat only; a `success == false` load is forgotten, not guessed at) and the timer both run `Vault.ResolvePending`, which runs `Vault.Reread` - `GetItemHyperlink` again, then the same item reads as the first pass - on the SAME record objects the panel was handed, and asks for ONE redraw per batch through `ns.UI.RefreshVault` (`C_Timer.After(0)`-coalesced). A record the client never describes stays `pending` in words, is dropped from `Vault.pendingItems` when the bound runs out, and is asked for again on the next `Options()`. `pendingRewards` and `requestedItems` ride on the result. **M5-1 (WKE-550) moved the ASKING into `ns.ItemData`** and left every rule about what is pending here: `Vault.pendingItems`, `Vault.PendingCount`, `Vault.OnItemData`, `Vault.ResolvePending`, `Vault.Reread`, `Vault.Forget` and the coalesced `ns.UI.RefreshVault` are unchanged in behaviour, `ITEM_DATA_WAIT_SECONDS` / `ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS` / `ITEM_DATA_EVENTS` are now aliases of ItemData's, and `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` LEFT `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES` with the call itself - a list that claims a call the file does not make is worth nothing. `FUNCTION_NAMES` now names the C_Item reads too (they were always called). **The activity shape is measured** (four snapshots, 2026-09-05 and 2026-09-06); **the reward shape is Blizzard's exported `WeeklyRewardActivityRewardInfo`, not measured** - `rewards` was empty in every committed snapshot bar the Concession currency - and WKE-523's after-reset capture is what confirms it | yes (`spec/vault_spec.lua`, three transcripts replayed through the stub: 09-06, 09-08 12:45, and the 09-09 08:59 fresh-login one, with the 09-08 23:26 snapshot of the same itemDBIDs replayed over it as the client's late answer) |
| `Modules/Currencies.lua` (shipped M3-9, read by ID in M3-11) | how many Catalyst charges and season crests the player has, for the two Vault-tab lines whose scenarios assumed them | `ns.Currencies.Read(opts)` -> `{ ok = true, source = "live"\|"capture", entries, byID, crests, crestsKnown, catalystKnown, catalystCharges, catalystMax, catalyst, secretsSeen }` or `{ ok = false, reason }`. The live client wins when it can be asked; in combat, or on a client with no `C_CurrencyInfo`, the newest stored `currencies` snapshot answers instead and `source` says so. `Currencies.FUNCTION_NAMES` lists the three calls, literally, because `C_CurrencyInfo` also carries the currency-transfer functions. **M3-11 (WKE-546) makes the currency ID the primary key**, because a by-name read of the list is a read of the currency TAB and the tab lists only the rows of expanded headers - which is how M3-9 concluded, from a transcript with 8 of 10 headers collapsed, that the Catalyst charge is not a currency. `Currencies.KNOWN_IDS = { crests = { 3442, 3443, 3444, 3445, 3446 }, catalyst = { 3465 } }`, every ID MEASURED (the crests from the 23:04 transcript's "Crests" header; 3465 from the owner's own Catalyst tooltip, "Venomblight Manaflux ... Total Maximum: 1/8, CurrencyID 3465") and none remembered from a wiki. `Read()` resolves each configured ID through the direct `GetCurrencyInfo` probe, then the list's info half, then - only when no ID answered - the list by name through `CREST_NAMES` / `CATALYST_NAMES`, which survive as that fallback and as the crest order; the name shown is always the client's own `CurrencyInfo.name`. `crestsKnown` / `catalystKnown` mean "an ID or a name is configured". `catalystCharges` and `catalystMax` are the client's `quantity` and `maxQuantity`, numbers or nil, and nil is never shown as 0. **`CATALYST_NOT_A_CURRENCY` is gone** - it was true of one night's scroll state and false about the client. **M5-4 (WKE-553) keeps the client's own `iconFileID`** on every record `Currencies.record` builds, and hands the whole Catalyst record on as `read.catalyst` beside the two numbers that were already there, because the Vault tab now draws each crest and the charge with its own icon and the client's own name. The issue's premise that M3-11 "already read" the icon was half true: the call was made, the field was dropped. Nothing is derived from an icon; it is a number the client handed over. **No arithmetic of any kind** - no cost, no total, no "enough for N upgrades"; an item's upgrade cost is not readable outside the upgrade UI and is not estimated | yes (`spec/currencies_spec.lua`; the `world.currencies` tests use labelled placeholders in Blizzard's documented `CurrencyInfo` shape, the fixture tests use the owner's real client. The one that carries the issue: a list whose headers are ALL collapsed, showing nothing, still yields the crests and the charge out of the by-ID probe) |
| `Modules/QEImport.lua` (shipped M2-1) | parse pasted JSON -> verdict; store it | `ns.QEImport.Parse(text)` -> `{ ok = true, verdict, warnings }` or `{ ok = false, reason }`. Verdict `{ exportedAt, spec, contentType, reportId, player, topSet = { score, stats, items = [itemKey], order = {itemKey...} }, alternatives = { {scorePercent, hpsDifference, items (a list), gems} }, vault = [itemKey], skippedItems }`; item `{ key, itemID, slot, level, bonusIDs (sorted), gems, enchant, tertiary, setId, isVault, isExclusive, source, count }`. Refuses: nothing pasted, non-JSON, non-object JSON, `schema ~= "qe-live-droptimizer"`, `version` that is not the number `1` (names both), missing `topSet`, `gameType ~= "Retail"` - each naming what it saw, strings clipped to 40 chars. Warns (never refuses) on a character-name mismatch, an empty topSet, and items skipped for a bad itemID. `Store`/`Current`/`Import` keep the last verdict in `db.char.qeImport` with `exportedAt` and `importedAt`. `AlternativeIsBetter(alt)` is the single reading of the sign convention. **C-6 (WKE-540) adds the named scenario as part of a Top Gear verdict's identity:** `SCENARIOS` is `asOffered`, `catalyzed`, `maxed` (the companion's strings verbatim), `DEFAULT_SCENARIO` is `asOffered` and `ScenarioKey(verdict)` reads a MISSING scenario as it - every paste and every file written before C-6 says nothing and every one of them is the character as it stands - while a name this build does not know gets `UNKNOWN_SCENARIO` rather than the default's shelf. `Store` files every verdict under `db.char.qeImportsByScenario[contentType][scenario]` and **writes `qeImport` / `qeImports[contentType]` only for `asOffered`**, which is the whole of "Equip Now and the Upgrade Map never change meaning"; `ForContentTypeAndScenario` and `Scenarios(contentType)` (ask-order, unknown names appended, a pre-C-6 verdict answered as `asOffered` when the shelf is empty) read them back, and `Existing(verdict)` compares like with like so three answers about one content type are not two repeats. **`CatalyzedCoverage(verdict, option)` is the second join, and the only reason the Catalyst question is answerable:** `autoCatalyze` does not change an item, it ADDS a clone (`SimCImportEngine.ts` ~253-265) whose `convertToTier` takes the tier piece's item ID and set ID and keeps the slot, level, bonus IDs and `vaultItem` flag, and his exporter carries neither `catalyzedID` nor `isCatalystItem` - so his clone is recognised by the fields he copied (same slot, same NON-EMPTY bonus IDs, still `isVault`, a different item ID) and by nothing else. It is a join, not a model: nothing here knows what can be catalyzed. **`CatalyzedOwned(verdict, inventory)` (M3-13) points that same join at the inventory scan** and lists every piece of his BEST SET that is his clone of an item the owner already owns, in his top-set order, guarded by the run's own `autoCatalyze`, by the exact key already being in the scan, and by there being a scan at all. **`OneChargeCandidates(verdict, inventory, vaultOptions)` (M3-14, WKE-555; the vault argument M3-15, WKE-556) answers the fifth question off his own alternatives:** the top set and each differential is a candidate SET - a differential names a slot completely (his `buildDifferential`, `TopGearEngine.ts:499-511`, pushes both rings or both trinkets the first time either differs), so applying one is "every item in a slot he names comes from him, every other slot keeps the top set's" - and the sets whose `CatalyzedOwned` count is exactly ONE come back ordered by `AlternativeRank`, each carrying his `scorePercent` / `hpsDifference` (0 for the top set, which is zero from itself by definition) and the single clone it spends the charge on. It chooses no item and adds no number. **M3-15 (WKE-556) fixes what "the charge" counts.** 555's issue said a one-charge set had exactly one catalyzed OWNED item and skipped every `isVault` item; the Catalyst spends a charge per item converted and does not care whose the item is. `conversionOf` is now the single place a set item is weighed: a tier item flagged `isVault` is a charge unless `ns.Vault.Options()` is offering that very item ID (a reward that arrives as a tier piece converts nothing), it is named through the same slot-and-bonus-IDs join `CatalyzedCoverage` uses, and with no vault snapshot it is counted unnamed - the conservative reading, because calling a conversion free is the only mistake worth avoiding. `CatalyzedOwned(verdict, inventory)` is unchanged and still owned-only by design; **`CatalyzedVault(verdict, vaultOptions)`** is its sibling for the other half, guarded by `autoCatalyze` alone because it reads no bags | yes, fully (`spec/qeimport_spec.lua`, 84 tests over real JSON text) |
| `Modules/UFImport.lua` (shipped M3-6) | parse a pasted Upgrade Finder export -> verdict; store it | `ns.UFImport.Parse(text)` -> `{ ok = true, verdict, warnings }` or `{ ok = false, reason }`. Verdict `{ schema, version, exportedAt, spec, contentType, reportId, player, settings (his `ufSettings`), items = ["<itemID>@<itemLevel>"], order, levelsByItemID, skippedItems }`; entry `{ key, itemID, level, slot, instanceID, encounterID, dropLoc, dropType, dropDifficulty, upgradePercent, hpsGain, score, count, sources }`. Refuses: nothing pasted, non-JSON, `schema ~= "qe-live-upgradefinder"` (naming both), `version` that is not the number `1`, missing `items`, `gameType ~= "Retail"`. Warns on a character-name mismatch, an export that ranks nothing, drops skipped for a bad itemID or level, and a drop listed twice with different values (the first is kept). **`UFImport.UPGRADE_BETTER_PERCENT_SIGN` and `UPGRADE_BETTER_HPS_GAIN_SIGN` are both `+1` and mean the OPPOSITE of `QEImport`'s `ALT_WORSE_*`** - the same number carrying the other meaning, which is why this is its own file (decision 2026-09-07). `Lookup(verdict, itemID, level)` answers only for the EXACT item level; `LevelsFor(verdict, itemID)` says which levels the export does carry, so a caller can tell silence from a level mismatch and never turn one into a number. A drop several drop types list is one entry with several `sources`, not several entries. `Store`/`Current`/`ForContentType`/`StoredContentTypes`/`Import` mirror `QEImport` over `db.char.ufImport` / `db.char.ufImports[contentType]`. **C-7 (WKE-543) adds the Mythic+ key level as part of a verdict's identity:** `KeyLevelOf(verdict)` reads `verdict.keyLevel` and insists on a whole non-negative NUMBER (the string "10" is not one - this decides a shelf, and a value that had to be converted is not one anybody wrote deliberately); `KeyLevelKey` is the lenient counterpart that normalises what a CALLER asks for, and answers `UNKNOWN_KEY_LEVEL` for anything that is not a level; `Store` also files under `db.char.ufImportsByLevel[contentType][keyLevel]`; `ForContentTypeAndLevel` and `StoredKeyLevels(contentType)` (ascending, plus whether one export does not say) read them back; `Existing(verdict)` is what `ns.Companion` asks instead of matching on content type. **M3-10 (WKE-545) replaced C-7's single-document pick with the cross-level join, and `PickForLevel` / `KeyLevelNote` / `PICK_*` are gone with it:** `Documents(contentType)` -> `{ { verdict, keyLevel } }` ascending with a document that names no level last (and a pre-C-7 `ufImports` entry as the whole list when the by-level shelf is empty) is **the one place the database is read**, and everything else is pure over that list; `LookupAcrossLevels(documents, itemID, level)` -> `entry, keyLevel, how, matches` keeps the EXACT `itemID@itemLevel` match wherever it is found and answers `nil` when no document carries it - never a neighbouring level, never an interpolation - with `MATCH_DROP` (QE Live's own `dropType` calls that level a `drop`, read off the entry and its `sources`, which is what `IsDropAtLevel` is for) beating `MATCH_LOWEST` (the lowest key level) when several carry it, and `MATCH_ONLY` when one does; `LevelsAcrossLevels` is `LevelsFor` over the whole set, so "QE Live has never seen this drop" stays distinguishable from "never at this level"; `KeyLabel(level)` is the only place a level becomes a word (`+6`, `+%d` and nothing else); `DocumentsNote` is the sentence the panel puts under its header (`QE Live's Upgrade Finder at +2, +4, +6, +8, +10 (5 documents).`). **Nothing here converts `settings.dungeon` into a key level**: that field is an index into QE Live's own table and turning it into a level would restate his data in Lua | yes, fully (`spec/ufimport_spec.lua`, 55 tests over real JSON text: the hand-built sample, the two 2026-09-07 exports, the five 2026-09-08 key-level documents and the Top Gear export as a refusal) |
| `Modules/Companion.lua` (shipped C-2) | read the one file the local companion writes (`Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua`, listed in the `.toc` after `Core.lua`), import each document it carries, and say where the verdict on screen came from | **The chunk's contract, which C-1 must write and nothing else may extend:** it assigns `ns.companionVerdict = { writtenAt = "<ISO 8601 UTC>", companionVersion = "<string>", qeSettings = { autoUpgradeVault = <bool>, autoUpgradeAll = <bool> }, exports = { { schema, contentType, json = "<QE Live's export text, verbatim>" }, ... } }` and does nothing else - Lua strings, numbers, booleans and tables only, no calls. `Companion.Validate(raw, now)` -> `{ok=true, writtenAt, writtenAtEpoch, companionVersion, qeSettings, exports}`, or `{ok=false, absent=true}` for the untouched placeholder (silent, not a refusal), or a refusal naming what it read. `Companion.Entry(raw, index)` refuses a non-table entry, a non-string `json`, one past `MAX_JSON_BYTES` (4 MB), a missing schema, and a schema **neither importer owns** - naming both, and never guessing at one. **Since M3-6 `Companion.ImporterFor(schema)` picks `ns.QEImport` or `ns.UFImport`**; before it, a `qe-live-upgradefinder` document was refused by name rather than handed to the Top Gear parser. `Companion.ImportAll(raw, now)` -> `{ok, imported, skipped, unchanged}`: each `json` goes through **its own importer's `Parse` and `Store`, the same path as a paste**, so every schema / version 1 / `gameType` refusal applies verbatim, and a refusal is reported without disturbing what is stored. This file calls exactly four things of an importer - `Parse`, `ContentTypeKey`, `ForContentType`, `Store` - and reaches past none of them into a verdict's shape, because the two shapes disagree and their signs are opposites. The staleness check therefore compares **like with like**: a Top Gear import is never made stale by an Upgrade Finder one for the same content type. The verdict is filed under **the content type inside the JSON**, never the entry's advisory `contentType`, and carries `source = "companion"`, `companionWrittenAt`, `companionVersion` and, **since M3-7, `qeSettings`** (per document since C-6, falling back to the file's) - the pair C-5 records, carried onto the verdict rather than left in the file's result because the Vault panel reads it off whichever verdict is on screen, which may have come back from SavedVariables long after that file was replaced. `Companion.Settings(raw)` is the only reader of it: `ns.Safe` first, then **both** of `autoUpgradeVault` / `autoUpgradeAll` must really be booleans or the whole pair is nil, because half a stated pair is not a setting; `autoCatalyze` (C-6) rides along when it really is a boolean and is left out otherwise, so a file that never mentions the Catalyst claims nothing about it. **C-6 also reads each entry's `scenario`**, sets it on the verdict BEFORE asking the importer what that verdict replaces (without which three Top Gear answers for one content type read as three repeats of the first, exactly the failure C-7 hit with key levels), and passes the string through unrepaired - `QEImport.ScenarioKey` is the one place a name becomes a shelf. A file that does not say (anything written before C-5, and the committed placeholder) still imports; it simply says nothing about assumptions. **The later of the two wins:** a file older than the stored import's `companionWrittenAt`/`importedAt` is skipped and said so; the same file read again on the next `/reload` is `unchanged` and silent. Every field passes `ns.Safe` before its type is checked, nothing in the chunk is called, there is no `loadstring`. `Companion.SourceText(verdict, now)` is the one phrase the window and `/lootpath status` show (`"pasted"` / `"companion, written N minute(s) ago"`). **`Companion.Refresh()` is `/lootpath refresh`, and since C-3 it captures before it reloads**: `Companion.REFRESH_CAPTURES` is `{ "env", "inventory", "vault" }` and each is run through `ns.RunCapture` in that order, so the three snapshots the companion's profile is built from are this session's. Any refusal (`combat`, the DB not loaded, another capture still running) is repeated verbatim in chat and **stops the reload**; a client with no `ReloadUI` is refused before anything is captured, because three snapshots nobody can flush are three snapshots written for nothing. `journal` is never among them - it is the one asynchronous capture, the SimC profile reads none of it, and `db.global.journalCache` is untouched by a refresh. `Companion.RefreshSummary(snapshots)` is the chat line's `gear, bags, bank (open/closed), vault`, and it reads the bank half back out of **the snapshot just taken** (`C_Bank.CanViewBank` for the character's own bank) rather than asking the client a second time that could disagree with what the companion will read. It **refuses in combat** (`InCombatLockdown`), where `ReloadUI` is protected and the captures refuse anyway | yes (`spec/companion_spec.lua`, 44 tests: a REAL Lua chunk built from the committed exports, compiled and loaded with the `.toc` loader's own varargs, then `ADDON_LOADED`; since M3-6 one file carrying both kinds for one content type is among them) |
