Row `{ seq, slot, equipped, best, verdictItem, status, matchedBy, reason, dstSlot }`. **One row per item in `topSet.order`**, in the export's own order, so a matched pair of rings is two rows; plus one `no_verdict` row per equipped item in a slot the export never names. **M2-4 (WKE-541): an unclaimed Great Vault option is its own status, `best_in_vault`, and not a gap** - it carries no `reason` because there is nothing to explain away, it keeps the `equipped` record the spare pass hands it (what you wear meanwhile), and it is counted apart in `counts.best_in_vault`. `best_not_owned` is now only the two meanings that really are gaps, and its `reason` distinguishes them: a **closed bank** the scan could not see into, and simply absent. Exact `ns.ItemKey` first; **itemID + item level** is the only fallback, it sets `matchedBy` and lands in `fallbacks` AND in the chat frame; an itemID-only match is never accepted. Every inventory record is claimed at most once, and an equipped copy beats a bag copy beats a bank copy. `dstSlot` is the **scanned** `slotIndex` of the item being replaced (nil when the slot is empty, and the client then chooses), which is what stops two rings fighting over one slot. `ns.Match.IsSwap(row)` is the single predicate the Equip button reads | yes (`spec/match_spec.lua`, over the 09-05 transcript joined to the genuine 09-06 export, the hand-built sample, and synthetic records for the fallback / vault / closed-bank cases) |
| `UI/ItemLine.lua` + `UI/EquipPanel.lua` + `UI/Options.lua` + `UI/MainFrame.lua` (shipped M2-2; the three tabs added in M3-3; the chrome M5-2; the item line and the redrawn Equip Now M5-1) | the one window: paste editbox, import status line, and **one tab per promise - Equip Now, Upgrade Map, Vault** | `ns.UI.Toggle()` on `/lootpath`; `UI.Frame()` builds once, named `LootpathMainFrame` so `UISpecialFrames` can close it on Escape. Templates: `BasicFrameTemplateWithInset` (its `TitleText`/`CloseButton` come from `BaseBasicFrameTemplate`) and `InputScrollFrameTemplate` (multiLine `EditBox` at parentKey `EditBox`, `SetMaxLetters(0)`, `CharCount` hidden because it counts DOWN from the limit). `UI.StatusText` shows **which kind was imported**, then spec, content type, `UI.AgeText(exportedAt)` and the count, or the parser's refusal **verbatim**; when the character also has the other kind for that content type it adds an `Also stored:` line with its age, so two runs are never mistaken for one. `UI.ActiveVerdict()` picks the Top Gear import matching `settings.contentType` and otherwise falls back to the most recent one, saying so; **`UI.ActiveUpgradeFinderDocuments()` (M3-6, reshaped by M3-10) does the same for the Upgrade Finder import** - kept as its own function so no caller can get both back at once and treat two opposite sign conventions as one thing - but hands back **every** document stored for that content type (`ns.UFImport.Documents`), never one pick of them, because since WKE-545 a drop takes its number from whichever key-level document values it at the item level the client lists. It answers an empty list, never nil. **`UI.ActiveVerdictScenarios()` (C-6) is the third of that family**: every stored `{ verdict, scenario }` for the content type on screen, its own function for the same reason - `ActiveVerdict` must keep answering `asOffered` alone, and a caller that could get the whole set back in one call could put a `maxed` answer on a tab that promises what you own now. **`UI.DetectSchema(text)` / `UI.ImportAny(text)` (M3-6)** route a paste: the schema string is read with a pattern rather than by decoding, because a 120 KB export should not be decoded twice, and nothing is trusted to that match - the parser it chooses re-checks schema, version and game type properly. Equip via `C_Item.EquipItemByName(link, dstSlot)` (Blizzard docs; the global `EquipItemByName` sits in Ketho's deprecated file - settled 2026-09-05). Options through `Settings.RegisterVerticalLayoutCategory` / `RegisterProxySetting` / `CreateDropdown` / `RegisterAddOnCategory`: one dropdown over `ns.QEImport.CONTENT_TYPES`, and since C-6 a second over `ns.QEImport.SCENARIOS` (`db.profile.settings.vaultScenario`, default `asOffered`) that moves the VAULT TAB'S HIGHLIGHT and nothing else. The panel draws a fixed list of at most 20 rows (a full set is 15-19) with the remainder counted, not dropped. **M5-1 (WKE-550): a row is a `UI.ItemLine`, not a font string.** The widget takes one shape - `{ itemID, link, name, quality, itemLevel, icon, second, badge = { text, tone, hex, atlas }, tags }` - and draws the icon (the caller's, else `ItemData.Instant`, else `INV_Misc_QuestionMark`), an `IconBorder` of `Interface\Common\WhiteIconFrame` tinted through `ColorManager.GetColorDataForItemQuality` then `C_Item.GetItemQualityColor` then `ITEM_QUALITY_COLORS` (each guarded; **no answer means no border, never a guessed one**), the item level bottom-right of the icon in `NumberFontNormalSmall`, the name in quality colour on one non-wrapping line, a grey second line, a text badge and tag words. A row the client cannot name yet says `RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO` and asks `ns.ItemData.Request`, cancelling the handle whenever the line is handed another item, so a late answer never lands on a re-used row; a row whose data never arrives stays exactly as it is. Hovering the icon or the name opens the item's own tooltip - `SetHyperlink` with a link, `SetItemByID` without one - with `GameTooltip_ShowCompareItem` behind a type check. **The badge is text, never a bar**, because a bar's length would be arithmetic on QE Live's numbers; its tones are his (`better` `#FFDF14`, `worse` `#C16719` at 0.72 alpha, `none`/`neutral` grey) and its tag colours are his words resolved through the CSS named-colour table (Vault aqua `00FFFF`, Catalyst plum `DDA0DD`, Tier yellow `FFFF00`, Exclusive orange `FFA500`, an unknown tag in grey rather than dropped). Every atlas is validated at runtime with `C_Texture.GetAtlasInfo` and has a word to fall back on. **Equip Now binds it:** `Describe` keeps `text` and `note` word for word and gains `name`, `second`, `badge`, `tags`, `item` and `worn`; a swap is worn icon -> `common-icon-forwardarrow` -> best icon with the Equip button, an already-best row is one icon with `common-icon-checkmark` in the badge, a `best_in_vault` row carries the Vault tag and keeps its note; the five counts are chips above the list (`EquipPanel.Chips`, pure) and `EquipPanel.NoteText` keeps only what the chips do not say, while `SummaryText` stays the whole line `/lootpath status` reads. Twenty item lines are 760 points against the window's body, so **the list became a `UIPanelScrollFrameTemplate`** rather than the panel deciding the window's size for M5-2. **M2-4 (WKE-541): `EquipPanel.Describe` returns `note` as well as `text`, and every sentence a row has to say lives on the note.** The detail line is one line that does not wrap, so anything long appended to it used to run off the frame; the note is a second font string under it, anchored LEFT and RIGHT to the row (no `SetWidth`, so its width is the frame's), `SetWordWrap(true)`, and the row grows by `EquipPanel.NOTE_HEIGHT` when it has one. A `best_in_vault` row shows the item you wear now as **already equipped** in its own blue - not the gap's red, and never `X -> Y (not found)` - with `EquipPanel.VaultNoteText` underneath in the panel's note grey: `QE Live's best set has a Great Vault option in this slot: <name> (QE Live's level <n>) - see the Vault tab`. It quotes QE Live's level and points at the tab; it computes nothing. A `best_not_owned` row keeps `(not found)` on the first line and puts the reason on the note. The summary line counts the vault apart: `N already best, N to swap, N waiting in the Great Vault, N not owned, N without a verdict`. The Equip button is anchored to the row's top rather than its middle so a two-line row still puts it beside the first line, and the overflow line anchors under the last row drawn rather than under the list frame's nominal height. **Tabs (M3-3):** `UI.TABS` is the three promises in the order §1 states them; the buttons are `PanelTabButtonTemplate` (`parentArray="Tabs"`, so they land in `frame.Tabs`) and `PanelTemplates_SetNumTabs`/`SetTab` are called through a `type(...) == "function"` check for the selected artwork ONLY - which panel is visible is `UI.ShowTab`'s own state, so a client without those helpers still tabs. **Title (M3-5):** `ns.VERSION` is the `.toc` metadata unless it still starts with `@`, which is the BigWigs packager's unsubstituted token on a dev copy - then it is `"dev"`. **`UI.Refresh` redraws only the tab on screen**, because rebuilding the journal map on every `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` would walk 613 rows for a panel nobody is looking at; `UI.RefreshEquip` is the Equip tab's half, split out for that. **`UI.RefreshVault` (M3-12)** is the third: the Vault module's second read calls it when a pending reward resolves, and it redraws the Vault tab only when the window is open on it (`UI.VAULT_TAB`), touching nothing else. Every tab's panel fills the same rectangle. **The chrome (M5-2, WKE-551):** the window is `PortraitFrameTemplate` (`PortraitFrameTemplateNoCloseButton` -> `PortraitFrameTexturedBaseTemplate` -> `PortraitFrameBaseTemplate`, which is where `PortraitContainer.portrait`, `TitleContainer` and `TitleText` come from; `CloseButton` is PortraitFrameTemplate's own), and it has **no inset frame**, so the panels anchor to the frame's own edges and the tabs hang off its `BOTTOMLEFT`. `UI.ApplyPortrait` fills the ring with `UI.SpecIcon()` - `C_SpecializationInfo.GetSpecialization`/`GetSpecializationInfo` first, the two deprecated globals second - and falls back to the class icon through `CLASS_ICON_TCOORDS`, leaving the ring empty rather than guessing when the client names neither; `PLAYER_SPECIALIZATION_CHANGED` redraws it and the minimap button's icon. **`UI.StatusStripModel(now)`** is the one line under the title - `QE Live | spec | contentType kind | source, age | vault pick` joined by `UI.SEPARATOR` - built from the same facts `UI.VerdictNoteText` states in a sentence and `ns.Companion.SourceText` names the source with; it returns `{ text, stale, fellBack, tooltip }` so the age can be toned amber without a test reading colour codes, and `stale` is `VaultPanel.IsVerdictStale` over the client's own `C_DateAndTime.GetSecondsUntilWeeklyReset` (nil, never true, when the client will not say). The sentence, the `Also stored:` line and why an age is amber live in the strip's tooltip, never on the line. **The Import dialog** is a `BasicFrameTemplateWithInset` frame (`LootpathImportDialog`, also in `UISpecialFrames`) built with the window and hidden: it holds the editbox, Import, Clear and the import status line, and `frame.pasteBox`/`importButton`/`clearButton`/`status` are aliases onto it, so `UI.Import`, `UI.ImportAny` and `UI.StatusText` are untouched. **The launcher** is `## AddonCompartmentFunc: LootpathToggle` in the `.toc` - the one global Lootpath defines, which Blizzard's `AddonCompartmentMixin` looks up in `_G` - plus a native minimap button (`UI.MinimapButton`, built in `onReady`) whose position is `UI.MinimapButtonOffset(angle)` at radius 80 and whose drag reads the angle back with `UI.MinimapAngleFrom`, saved in `db.profile.settings.minimapAngle`. **Two settings** join the two dropdowns: `Settings.CreateSlider` over `Settings.CreateSliderOptions(0.7, 1.3, 0.05)` for `settings.scale` (clamped reading AND writing, applied with `frame:SetScale` on the window alone) and `Settings.CreateCheckbox` for `settings.compactRows`, which is stored for M5-1's item line and draws nothing yet. Both control builders are guarded separately from the category, so a client with `RegisterProxySetting` but no `CreateSlider` keeps the dropdowns | yes (`spec/ui_spec.lua`, over the stub's widget model: clicks reach `QEImport.Parse`, a 200 KB paste round-trips, `C_Item.EquipItemByName` is called with the link and the scanned slot, combat disables everything; and `spec/itemline_spec.lua` since M5-1, which asserts the icon file ID, the border's tint, the badge string and the tags per row over the 2026-09-05 scan and the 2026-09-07 Dungeon export - the stub records what a texture was told to show since that issue). What it LOOKS like is M2-3 and M5-5, in game |
| `UI/UpgradeMapPanel.lua` (shipped M3-3, by-run view M3-8) | the loot map, two ways: per gear slot (what is worn, then every spec-appropriate drop the journal listed) and per run (where to go) | `Panel.Model(opts)` is pure over `{ sources, summary, inventory, verdict, difficultyIDs }` -> `{ note, slots = { { slot, equipped, candidates, hiddenLevelOne, hiddenNote } }, difficulties, pending = { count, note, rows }, counts }`; `Panel.Lines(model)` is the display text and does **not** repeat the pinned note the header already draws (M3-5); `Panel.Create/Refresh` the frames. **M3-5:** `Panel.DifficultyLabels(ids, previewLevel)` labels the whole map's difficulties at once and gives no two the same words - `GetDifficultyInfo` answers the bare localised name for dungeon Heroic (2) and raid Heroic (15) alike, so a colliding pair falls back to `Panel.DIFFICULTY_NAME`'s qualified forms and, failing that, to the ID; the set is the whole map's, so a filter never renames a button. `Panel.FilterLayout(labels, width, measure)` is the pure greedy packing that wraps the filter row instead of running it off the frame, measured by the button's own font string in the client and by a per-character estimate headlessly; `Panel.Refresh` re-anchors the buttons and moves the list down by `filterRows`. A candidate whose client-reported item level is exactly `Panel.HIDDEN_ITEM_LEVEL` (1) is not listed; the slot ends with `Panel.LEVEL_ONE_NOTE` and the count. `counts.hiddenLevelOne` keeps the total, so nothing is dropped silently; `Panel.Gather` reads the newest stored journal walk (`db.global.captures.journal`, which today only `/lootpath capture journal` writes), the inventory, and the verdict **through `ns.UI.ActiveVerdict()`** - the window's content-type setting decides which import a panel shows, and reaching past it to `QEImport.Current()` would answer a Dungeon setting with a Raid export and say nothing about it. **There are exactly two paths to a number, both QE Live's own, and no third.** `row.value` is nil unless `row.qe` is, and `row.qe` is `ns.QEImport.Coverage(verdict, row.itemKey)` on the EXACT item key - the vault and the gear you own. `row.upgradeValue` (M3-6) is nil unless `row.upgrade` is, and `row.upgrade` is `ns.UFImport.LookupAcrossLevels(documents, row.itemID, row.itemLevel)` on the EXACT itemID + item level - the drops you do not own. **Since M3-10 (WKE-545) `documents` is EVERY Upgrade Finder document stored for the content type**, read through `ns.UI.ActiveUpgradeFinderDocuments()` and normalised by `Panel.UpgradeDocuments` (a single `opts.upgrades` verdict becomes a one-document list, so there is one join path and not two); the row keeps `upgradeKeyLevel` and `upgradeKeyPick` and `Panel.UpgradeText(entry, keyLevel)` puts the document's key label in the sentence (`QE Live: better by 1.83% (at +6)`), because on one screen two rows can come from two of his runs. `model.upgradeDocumentsNote` (`ns.UFImport.DocumentsNote`) names the whole set above the rows. `Panel.UpgradeText` renders his percentage and reads the direction from `ns.UFImport.IsUpgrade`, never from arithmetic here; a zero says `no change` rather than a direction he did not give, and `hpsGain` is carried in the model but never put on a row. A row whose itemID he ranked at ANOTHER item level shows nothing, sets `row.rankedAtAnotherLevel`, and is counted in `counts.rankedAtAnotherLevel` with one deduplicated entry in `model.upgradeLevelMismatches` and a `Panel.LEVEL_MISMATCH_NOTE` line - counted, never estimated. A pending row is never joined at all, because its level is unknown rather than wrong. `row.owned` is ownership from the scanner, a fact and never a ranking. A pending row has no slot, so it is listed under `pending` as unknown - never as item level 0. **M3-8 (WKE-542) adds a SECOND model beside it, not a mode inside it:** `Panel.RunModel(opts)` takes what `Model` takes plus `opts.runSort`, and `Panel.RunLines(model)` is its display; the slot view's own rendering is byte for byte what it was. A **run** is `(instance, difficulty)` for a dungeon - the whole dungeon, because that is what you queue for - and `(instance, boss, difficulty)` for a raid, and `Panel.RunKey` puts the key level in a Mythic Keystone run's identity, so one dungeon at two key levels is two runs that sort against each other. `Panel.RunKeyLevel(difficultyID, previewLevel, entry)` reads `entry.mythicPlusLevel` when a walk carries one and the walk's single previewed level otherwise - and nothing writes that field today, because C-7 moved the several-key-levels axis onto QE LIVE's side rather than the walk's (§7). **C-7 (WKE-543), as M3-10 (WKE-545) left it:** `Panel.Gather` reads every stored document through `ns.UI.ActiveUpgradeFinderDocuments()` and both models turn the list into `model.upgradeDocumentsNote` through `ns.UFImport.DocumentsNote`, which both line builders draw - in the run view after `model.keyLevelNote`, because the key the WALK previewed (still one level, the Adventure Guide's own preview) and the keys QE LIVE was run at are two different facts and both are said. A run's rated drops are the cross-level matches, which is what makes a dungeon run rankable at all. It walks `sources` itself rather than regrouping `Model`'s output, because **the denominator has to be every drop the journal lists for that run** - the cosmetics the slot view hides and the rows whose item data never arrived included - and a filtered list cannot say that. `run.rated` counts only drops `ns.UFImport.IsUpgrade` calls upgrades, over the same exact itemID + item level join and no other; `run.best` is the largest of those and `run.bestText` carries HIS sign and magnitude with no direction word of its own. Two orders, `Panel.SORT_BEST` and `Panel.SORT_COUNT`, and a run with nothing rated sorts last under both as a consequence of them rather than as a clause of its own. `model.headline` restates the top row as one sentence; `Panel.RUN_NOTE` says on screen that neither ranking is weighted by drop chance; `model.keyLevelNote` names the key levels shown. The frame gains a view row above the difficulty row - `frame.modeButtons` (`By slot` / `By run`) and `frame.sortButtons`, the button for what is on screen disabled and the sort pair hidden outside the run view - while the difficulty filter, the pinned note and the combat refusal are shared by both views **M5-3 (WKE-552) redraws both views and changes no number.** `Panel.Lines` and `Panel.RunLines` are byte for byte what they were - they are what `/lootpath status` and every render test read - and beside them `Panel.Elements(model, state)` and `Panel.RunElements(model, state)` are pure lists of what is DRAWN: one entry per row, each naming its `kind` (`section` / `item` / `note` / `run`), its `height` and the model table it binds. The frame hands that list to a `WowScrollBoxList` through `CreateDataProvider`, so the walk's 478 drops cost the frames that fit the box and not one per drop: Blizzard's list view acquires frames only for the data indices on screen and releases the rest to a pool (`ScrollBoxListView`'s `ValidateDataRange`, read under `.luals/`), which the stub models and a test asserts. The heights live in the data because the box asks for an element's extent before it has a frame to measure. `state` is `Panel.CollapseState(db)` - `db.char.upgradeMap.collapsedSlots` and `.expandedRuns`, per character, because which slot matters is about the character. A slot section is drawn open or shut either way and always says how many drops are inside; a run card is shut until it is opened, because 48 runs of a dozen drops each is a list nobody can scan and the card already says the two facts the sort is about. `Panel.FinishRow(row)` fills in what a drawn row shows and computes nothing new: `row.second` is `Panel.SourceSecondText` (`boss - instance, difficulty`, the same three facts the printed line carries in its own order), `row.tags` is `{ "owned" }` for the ownership fact the printed line writes as `[owned 305]`, and `row.badge` is one of QE Live's own sentences. `Panel.ValueText` and `Panel.UpgradeText` are now the TEXT HALVES of `Panel.ValueBadge` and `Panel.UpgradeBadge`, so a drawn badge and a printed line cannot drift: the badge carries `text` (his verdict), `note` (`(at +6)`, which stored document said it, always grey because it is not one of his numbers) and `tone`, and `UpgradeText` is `text .. " " .. note`. The tone is `better` / `worse` from `ns.UFImport.IsUpgrade` or `coverage.isBetter` and `none` for his zero and for a row he did not rank - a top-set item is `neutral`, a status word rather than a number of his to colour. A row carrying BOTH numbers gives the badge to the Upgrade Finder verdict (it is the one about THIS drop at THIS item level) and puts the Top Gear sentence on the second line, so neither is dropped. A run gains `run.badge` (`best +1.83%`, his gold, because only a drop his own `IsUpgrade` calls an upgrade ever reaches a run's list) and `run.instanceImage`. **The difficulty filter is one `WowStyle1FilterDropdownTemplate`** built with the 11.0 menu API (`SetupMenu`, `rootDescription:CreateRadio`; `UIDropDownMenu` is deprecated and unused here), whose rows are `Panel.DifficultyOptions(model)` - the map's own difficulties, in the model's order and with the model's labels and counts, plus an all row. **`Panel.FilterLayout` and `Panel.EstimateLabelWidth` are gone with the buttons they packed**, and so are their tests: WKE-530 finding 2 was five buttons overflowing the window, and one dropdown holds any number of difficulties in the width of one control. The whole thing is a `pcall(CreateFrame, "DropdownButton", ...)`: a client without the template loses the dropdown on the tab and nothing else | yes (`spec/vaultpanel_spec.lua`; the M3-12 block replays vault snapshot 13 of the 2026-09-09 transcript, asserts no line carries `[]` or `nil`, and drives the frame through the second read; the M3-7 block replays vault snapshot 9 of the 2026-09-08 transcript against the companion's own export) |

| `tools/companion/` (C-1, WKE-533, plus C-4; NOT shipped in the addon zip - `.pkgmeta` ignores `tools`) | the local companion: SavedVariables -> SimC profile -> QE Live in the owner's fork -> `Data/QEVerdict.lua` | `companion.js` is the CLI (`--watch`, `--profile-only`, `--config`, `--out`, `--force`) and owns the exit codes (0 ok, 1 usage/config, 2 no SavedVariables, 3 profile incomplete, 4 fork unreachable, 5 QE Live refused, 6 write failed). **`lib/lua-savedvariables.js` and `lib/simc-profile.js` are S-2's, lifted out of `tools/companion-spike/` rather than copied**: C-1 wrote its own pair first, in parallel, and threw them away when S-2 merged, because S-2's mirrors the SimulationCraft addon's own offsets and slot tables, reproduces the client's `# Checksum:` with adler32, and enforces QE Live's two line-index rules itself. `lib/profile.js` is the thin part C-1 adds: one `{ ok, text, warnings, counts }` shape so a bad transcript is a named exit code rather than a stack trace, and the spec check. `lib/fork.js` is S-1's driver with a persistent browser profile (the welcome dialog is answered once) and `npm start` when nothing answers; **since C-5 (WKE-539) it also sets QE Live's two upgrade checkboxes itself before Submit** - `CHECKBOX_LABELS` names all three by the JSX string literal each is rendered with (`SimCraftDialog.js` lines 122-133; they are NOT `translate.json` keys), the two it was asked about are clicked only when they are wrong, both are read back, a click that did not take or a label that is not on the page is a `DRIVE` failure (exit 4) rather than a guess, `autoCatalyze` is never touched, and `settingsFrom()` reduces the per-box detail the log wants to the two booleans the writer records. **Since C-6 (WKE-540) the driver's unit of work is a PASS, not a document**: `configLib.plannedPasses(config, { hasVaultGear, force })` hands it an import plus the documents that import can answer, and `run()` re-imports the profile at the head of every pass because the boxes act at import. `lib/luawriter.js` renders the chunk; `lib/output.js` writes it to a temp file beside the target and renames, so the client never reads half a file and a failed run leaves the last good verdict alone; `lib/watch.js` gives one run per `/reload` (debounce plus two stats that agree on the size). **`lib/fingerprint.js` is C-4's (WKE-537): the profile decides whether QE Live is asked at all.** Since C-6 it also hashes the named scenario list (adding a scenario is a new question and must cost a run; reordering one must not). Since C-5 it hashes **the profile and the two QE Live upgrade settings together** - a canonical `# qeSettings autoUpgradeAll=... autoUpgradeVault=...` line appended to the kept lines, keys sorted - because the same gear asked about with `autoUpgradeVault` flipped is a different answer. It hashes the built SimC profile with four volatile lines removed - the identity line and `# Inventory captured ...` (both carry the capture stamp), `# Built by ...` (it names the tool, not the character) and `# Checksum:` (adler32 over the body, so it follows the stamps) - and remembers `{ hash, writtenAt, verdict }` in `<stateDir>/last-profile.json` after each verdict is safely written, never before. `once()` skips the QE Live run when the hash matches, the remembered path is the file this run would write, and that file still exists; `--force` overrides, and a missing, unreadable or hash-less state file runs and says why, because forgetting must cost a run and never skip one. The profile is what is hashed, not the SavedVariables: anything the profile does not carry cannot change QE Live's answer. The fork driver is injectable through `once(config, log, args, deps)` for exactly one reason - so a guard can prove the browser was NOT opened. **The file it writes is C-2's contract, not its own:** `ns.companionVerdict = { writtenAt, companionVersion, profileCapturedAt, qeSettings = { autoUpgradeVault, autoUpgradeAll } (C-5, booleans; the file-level pair, and since C-6 the Upgrade Finder's), exports = { { schema = "qe-live-droptimizer"|"qe-live-upgradefinder", contentType = "Dungeon"|"Raid", keyLevel (C-7, Upgrade Finder only), scenario (C-6, Top Gear only, one of the three names), qeSettings = { autoUpgradeVault, autoUpgradeAll, autoCatalyze } (C-6, per document, read back off his page), bytes, json } ... } }` - the writer REFUSES a Top Gear document with no scenario and an Upgrade Finder document that claims one, for the reason it refuses a key level on the wrong kind: a document filed under the wrong question is a wrong answer that looks right - `json` is QE Live's export text verbatim, so `ns.Companion` feeds it to the same `ns.QEImport.Parse` a paste goes through and every pin and refusal still applies | yes: 130 tests on Node's own runner (`node --test` in `tools/companion`, a sixth CI job and a `check.ps1` gate), S-2's 32 profile guards among them (they were in no gate before), the profile measured against the owner's real `/simc` string, and the writer's golden loaded by a real Lua interpreter in `spec/companionfile_spec.lua`, C-4's 14 guards driving the real `once()` over a fake `_retail_` in the OS temp directory with an injected fork driver (nothing near the real game folder, no browser), and C-5's checkbox step against a fake page that records every click and answers `isChecked` the way his controlled MUI checkboxes do. The fork driver itself is not mocked - it is proven by a recorded run (§9) |

**SavedVariables (AceDB):** `db.char.qeImport` (the most recent Top Gear verdict + exportedAt), **`db.char.qeImports[contentType]`** (one verdict per QE Live content type, so a Raid paste does not lose the Dungeon one; since C-2 a verdict also carries `source`, and a companion one `companionWrittenAt` / `companionVersion`, so where it came from survives a reload), **`db.char.ufImport` and `db.char.ufImports[contentType]`** (the same pair for Upgrade Finder exports, M3-6 - separate keys, so neither kind can overwrite the other, and a companion one carries the same `source` fields), **`db.char.qeImportsByScenario[contentType][scenario]`** (C-6: one Top Gear verdict per named scenario, with `"unknown"` as the shelf for a name this build does not know; `qeImport` and `qeImports` are written for `asOffered` alone, so everything that read them before C-6 keeps reading what the character has now), **`db.char.ufImportsByLevel[contentType][keyLevel]`** (C-7: one Upgrade Finder verdict per Mythic+ key level, with the string `"unknown"` as the shelf for an export that does not say which key it was run at; `ufImports` is untouched beside it and still holds the most recent verdict for the content type, so everything written before C-7 keeps reading what it always read - **the cost is that a five-level refresh stores five dungeon verdicts rather than one**, which is the feature and not an accident), `db.global.journalCache[key]`, `db.profile.settings.vaultScenario` (C-6, one of `ns.QEImport.SCENARIOS`; **its default is `thisWeek` since M3-13**, and it moves the Vault tab's highlight only - `qeImport` / `qeImports` stay the `asOffered` shelves whatever it says), `db.profile.settings.contentType` (**`"Dungeon"` or `"Raid"` - QE Live's own strings; it read `"Mythic+"` until M2-2 and could never have matched an export**). Captures write raw returns under a `captures` table pulled by `tools/sync.ps1 -Pull`.

**Libraries (all licence-checked, recorded in `Lootpath/Libs/LICENSES.md`):** LibStub (public domain), CallbackHandler-1.0 and AceDB-3.0 (Ace3 BSD-style, clause quoted in LICENSES.md: embedding permitted, "redistribution of a stand alone version is strictly prohibited"), rxi/json.lua 0.1.2 (MIT; vendored verbatim plus a six-line footer that sets `ns.json`, because the WoW loader discards a chunk's return value). Ace3 arrives as packager externals from the wowace SVN trunks in CI and from the WoWUIDev/Ace3 GitHub mirror locally (`tools/fetch-libs.ps1`); both directories are gitignored. Nothing else without a licence line.

**Test harness (M0-1):** `spec/stubs/wow.lua` installs a fake client into `_G` (shapes from Blizzard's docs, build tuple from the spike capture; placeholders, never wiki) and `spec/helpers/addon.lua` loads the `.toc` file list in order with `(addonName, ns)` varargs; a fake `LibStub("AceDB-3.0")` deep-copies the defaults. Fixtures under `spec/fixtures/` win over the stub whenever they disagree.

---

## 7. Decisions log (dated; do not reopen without a new entry here)

- **2026-09-09 (M5-4, WKE-553) - the Vault tab is drawn as Blizzard draws the vault, and every word of it still comes from somewhere.** Four things were decided while building it and are worth not reopening. **(a) The grid is a second VIEW of `Panel.Lines`, never a second answer.** The cell's verdict line, its hover, its level wording and its pick are the strings and the objects the text half already produced - `reward.verdictLines`, `reward.levelText`, `model.best` - so the two cannot disagree about what QE Live said, and `Panel.Lines` did not change by a byte. The drawn rows print `Panel.NoteLines` and `Panel.Lines` begins with the same function, which is what keeps that true as the notes grow. **(b) Blizzard's words are asked for at runtime and never written down.** The row order and the threshold patterns are read off Blizzard's shipped source, but the STRINGS - `RAIDS`, `DUNGEONS`, `WORLD`, `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_*` - are globals whose values nothing in this repo has a transcript of, so the panel asks `_G` for each and falls back to the labels measured off the owner's own vault screen. The stub defines none of them on purpose: the fallback is the path a test can exercise honestly, and one test defines sentinel values to prove the global wins. The same rule covers the selected atlas, which is validated with `C_Texture.GetAtlasInfo` and has a gold border behind it. **(c) A pick that is not a pick does not glow.** The headline has said `none - nothing in the vault beats your set` since M3-9; a glowing cell under that sentence would be the screen contradicting itself, so the test the headline makes is computed once, carried as `model.headline.closest`, and the closest option gets a grey label instead. **(d) A currency icon is a fact the client handed over, so it is kept.** `Currencies.record` was dropping `iconFileID` while M3-11's call was fetching it; the field is now kept and the whole Catalyst record handed on. Nothing is derived from an icon, and the counts beside the scenario lines are unchanged - the strip names the currencies, it does not add a number.
- **2026-09-09 (M5-3, WKE-552) - the two lists are one data provider, the collapse state is per character, and the walk records the art it was already being told.** Four things were settled while drawing the Upgrade Map. (1) **`Panel.Lines` and `Panel.RunLines` do not become the drawing.** They stay the pure text `/lootpath status` and every render test read, and `Panel.Elements` / `Panel.RunElements` are a SECOND pure function beside them describing what is drawn. Two functions over one model is more code than one; it is also the only arrangement in which a redraw cannot change what the addon says, and every values-free guard written since M3-3 keeps its teeth without being rewritten. (2) **One frame TYPE for four element kinds.** The scroll box's factory takes a frame type or an XML template and pools by it, and this addon ships no XML, so the four kinds cannot have four pools; each element frame therefore builds the widgets of whichever kinds it has been asked to be and hides the rest. The alternative - shipping an XML file for the first time to get four templates - buys nothing a reader can see and costs a build step. (3) **A slot section is open by default and a run card is shut by default.** The slot view answers "what could fill this", and its answer is the rows; the run view answers "where should I go", and its answer is on the card, so 48 runs of a dozen drops each would bury it. Both are remembered in `db.char.upgradeMap`, per character rather than per account, because which slot the owner cares about is a fact about the character. (4) **The walk records `buttonImage1`, `buttonImage2` and `bgImage`, and a card with no art draws no art.** `EJ_GetInstanceInfo` is already in `JournalAdapter.FUNCTION_NAMES` and only reads; keeping more of what it returns is not a new client action (CLAUDE.md's capture rule). The positions were read from Blizzard's own shipped Encounter Journal under `.luals/` rather than from the wiki - `name, _, _, icon = EJ_GetInstanceInfo(id)` at line 2413 fixes the fourth return as the button art, and line 1211's `instanceName, description, bgImage, _, loreImage, buttonImage, dungeonAreaMapID` fixes the three around it. All six committed transcripts predate the recording, so every one of them answers nil and the cards ship without art until the owner runs `capture journal` again (M5-5, human-required). A plain dark strip is what a card with no art gets: a stand-in picture of some other instance would be the panel saying something the client did not.

- **2026-09-09 (M5-1, WKE-550) - there is ONE item widget and ONE item loader, and the badge on either is text.** Three tabs were about to grow three ways of drawing an item and two ways of waiting for one. Decided: `UI/ItemLine.lua` is the only place an item becomes pixels and `Modules/ItemData.lua` is the only place the client is asked to load one - M3-12's request-and-re-read lifted out of `Modules/Vault.lua` unchanged in behaviour, with `Vault` keeping every rule about what is pending and `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` leaving `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES` with the call. Three consequences worth writing down. **(a) The badge is text, never a bar.** A bar's length is a number divided by a maximum, and both would be ours; QE Live's export carries a formatted string and the widget colours it and stops. **(b) QE Live's colours are read, not remembered.** `upgradeColor` in his `ItemUpgradeCard.js` (line 134) returns `#FFDF14` above zero and `#C16719` otherwise and that file's downgrade card is `opacity: 0.72`; his tag words are `getItemTags` in `MiniItemCard.tsx` (line 85) and are CSS colour NAMES - aqua, plum, yellow, orange, lightblue, lime - whose hex was resolved through the `color-name` table in the same checkout rather than from memory. No code or asset of his is copied; a colour is a fact about a look. **(c) A row that cannot be named is still a row.** The icon is `GetItemInfoInstant`'s fifth return, which is static data and answers before a load, so a pending row keeps its own icon and says `RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO` - Blizzard's own string, in Blizzard's own situation - and a load that never arrives leaves it exactly there. The corollary is the cancel: a line handed another item drops its request, or a late answer would fill in a row that has moved on. The stub now records `SetTexture` / `SetAtlas` / `SetVertexColor` / `SetAlpha` / `SetTextColor`, because which icon and which tint a row got are decisions, not pixels; how they LOOK is still the owner's eye test.

- **2026-09-09 (M5-2, WKE-551) - the chrome is Blizzard's portrait frame, the paste box is a dialog, and the launcher costs no library.** Five things were settled while building it. (1) **The portrait is filled by `UI.ApplyPortrait` rather than by `PortraitFrameMixin:SetPortraitToSpecIcon`.** Read under `.luals/` (`Blizzard_SharedXML/PortraitFrame.lua:78`), the mixin IS those two steps - the spec's icon, the class icon when there is no spec - so calling it would change nothing about the result and would put the only path to a portrait behind a FrameXML mixin the headless harness does not model. Doing it here makes the spec read, the class fallback and the "names neither" case one guarded piece of code a test drives. (2) **`C_SpecializationInfo` first, the globals second.** Blizzard's own annotations mark `GetSpecialization` and `GetSpecializationInfo` deprecated in favour of the namespaced pair (`Blizzard_Deprecated/Deprecated_Specialization_Standard.lua`); the issue named the globals, so both are asked, in that order, and neither is required. (3) **The strip returns a model, not a string.** The amber age is a colour code, and a test that asserted on `|cffffd43b` inside a sentence would be a test of the sentence; `{ text, stale, tooltip }` lets the staleness be asserted as a fact and the colour as a consequence. **A client that will not say when the weekly reset is gets `nil`, never `true`**: "we cannot tell how old this is" must not read as "this is stale". (4) **The paste box moved whole, and the window's four keys became aliases onto the dialog's widgets.** `UI.Import` writes to `UI.frame.status` and has since M2-2; making the dialog's status font string be that key means the import path, its routing and every one of its refusals are byte-for-byte what they were, which is what makes "the dialog changed nothing about importing" a claim rather than a hope. (5) **The launcher is native.** `## AddonCompartmentFunc` names a global function and costs nothing; `LibDBIcon`/`LibDataBroker` would need vendoring and a licence line in `Libs/LICENSES.md` (§8) for one user's minimap button. `LootpathToggle` is therefore the ONLY global the addon defines besides the slash handler. **Not decided here, because M5-0 (WKE-549) has not answered:** the window size stays 620 x 640 (the mockups are 760) and no ElvUI skin hook was built.
- **2026-09-09 (M3-12, WKE-547) - a vault reward the client has not loaded yet is `pending`, is asked for with `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID`, is read again on the client's load event, and is never printed as `[]`.** Measured on the 08:59 transcript (§9): right after a client restart `GetItemHyperlink` answers at once with `|h[]|h`, `GetItemInfo` / `GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` answer nothing, and the key is intact - the journal's 2026-09-06 second stage, on the vault. **Decided, and why each way:** (1) the exported function and the two documented events (`ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT`, `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED`, both `itemID, success` in Blizzard's Event.lua) rather than `Item:CreateFromItemID(id):ContinueOnItemLoad(fn)`. Read under `.luals/`, the mixin IS that call: `ItemEventListener:AddCallback` runs `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID(id)` once per id and fires callbacks on `ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` when `success` is true (`Blizzard_ObjectAPI/AsyncCallbackSystem.lua`, `Item.lua`). Calling it directly keeps every client call a literal name in `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES`, keeps the bound and the combat check in the module where a test drives them, and takes no dependency on a FrameXML global (`Item`) the headless harness does not model. The 2026-09-06 journal decision NOT to call `RequestLoadItemDataByID` stands for the journal: there, Blizzard's own loot path never calls it and 351 events fired unprompted; here, nothing fires unprompted - the vault's links sat nameless for the whole 08:59 capture - and the mixin Blizzard ships for exactly this case makes the same call. (2) The bound is the journal's (8 x 0.25 s) and a record that never resolves stays `pending` in words and is asked for again on the next look; it is never filled from anywhere else. (3) The panel may show the name from the newest stored vault snapshot that names the same `itemDBID`, labelled `(from the last capture)` - a fact the client stated in an earlier session about the same reward - and never the level from it, because a level on that row reads as the client's current answer. (4) The issue's "slot unknown" was not what the transcript says: `GetItemInfoInstant` (static data) answered the equip location on every pending reward, so the slot is kept and a pending reward is counted, valued and eligible for the pick like any other; a pending reward with no static data at all stays with the gear rather than becoming an extra, because "not known yet" is not "not gear". (5) Captures do not request: `capture vault` records the nameless link as it stands, and the module asks at panel time, out of combat.
- **2026-09-08 (M3-10, WKE-545) - the client says at what item level a drop drops, QE Live says what that item is worth at each level he modelled, and Lootpath joins on both.** C-7 filed a document per key level and the panel picked ONE of them, the one the walk's preview level named. Measured on real documents that ranked no dungeon row at all: his +10 dungeon rows come back at 311 and the client's keystone-10 preview lists the same drops at 305 (§9). **Decided:** the join searches every stored document for the content type and keeps the exact `itemID@itemLevel` match wherever it is found - which is his **+6** document - and the row says which document answered. Three things this deliberately is NOT: it does not adjust either side's item level, it does not take a neighbouring level or interpolate between two documents, and it does not infer a key level from an item level (that would be Lootpath restating his `MPLUS_KEY_REWARDS` table in Lua, which C-7 already refused). A drop no document carries at the client's level stays unranked and is counted, exactly as before. **The tie, when several documents carry one drop at one level** (his +6 and +7 buttons both end at 305; every dungeon document carries the same raid rows): QE Live's own `dropType` first - the document that calls that level a `drop` rather than an `Upgraded` or `Bonus Roll` listing - then the lowest key level. Both are his facts or the list's order, neither is a judgement about his numbers. **Which side of the six-level disagreement is stale is still not Lootpath's to decide** and goes to WKE-528.

- **2026-09-08 (C-7, WKE-543) - the key level axis belongs to QE Live's engine, and the addon files what it is told rather than deriving anything.** "Which dungeon at the lowest possible key level gives me an upgrade" cannot be read out of one Upgrade Finder export: his engine values every dungeon drop at the single key `ufSettings.dungeon` names. Decided: the companion asks him once per level (`upgradeFinderKeyLevels`, default `[2, 4, 6, 8, 10]`), every Upgrade Finder document records the level it was run at, and `ns.UFImport` files one verdict per `(contentType, keyLevel)`. Four details were settled while building it.
  (a) **A key level is not `ufSettings.dungeon`.** That field is an INDEX into `MPLUS_KEY_REWARDS` (`src/Databases/MPlusKeyRewards.ts`): `M0, +2/3, +4, +5, +6, +7, +8/9, +10` are indices 0..7, and index 7 gives 311 / 321 / 334. The issue read it as a level and proposed `[2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12]`; **there is no +12 in his table**, so the default is `[2, 4, 6, 8, 10]` and a level his page does not offer is a named failure rather than a nearest match.
  (b) **The mapping is read off his page, never restated.** `lib/fork.js` reads the toggle LABELS in the "Mythic+ Key Level" Paper, parses each into the levels a player would say (`+8/9` -> 8 and 9), and clicks the one covering the level asked for. Position in that row is his `key.index`, and that is not assumed either: every run compares the export's own `settings.dungeon` to the position it clicked and **fails the whole run (exit 4)** if they disagree, because a document filed under the wrong key level is a wrong answer that looks right.
  (c) **The addon does not derive a level from an export.** WKE-543 asked for a document without `keyLevel` to be filed under the level its `settings.dungeon` names; that is the index -> level table again, in Lua, where it would rot the day he adds a key. Instead such a document - a paste, or a file written before C-7 - is filed on an `"unknown"` shelf and the panel says the export does not say which key it was run at. **Rejected: guessing, and rejected: refusing the export.**
  (d) **The Raid Upgrade Finder document records a key level too**, against the issue's "the Raid one none". Measured on the committed 2026-09-07 pair: the Raid export carries the same 201 dungeon-sourced rows as the Dungeon one, all stamped `dropDifficulty: 7` at 311 / 321 / 334. A document whose rows were valued at a key is filed under that key; it simply runs once, at the highest configured level, rather than once per level, because the raid rows do not move with the key (measured: 156 of 156 non-dungeon rows are identical between the +2 and +10 runs).
  Also decided: the level list is hashed with the profile and the import settings (C-4/C-5), so adding or dropping a level costs a run and reordering one does not; and `ns.Companion` asks the importer what a verdict replaces (`importer.Existing(verdict)`) rather than matching on content type, because five dungeon documents in one file would otherwise read as four repeats of the first and be thrown away - a failure that is quiet, total, and was proven red before it was fixed.

- **2026-09-08 (C-3, WKE-536) - `/lootpath refresh` takes the three snapshots itself, and the reload is what flushes them.** The companion's profile is built from `newestSnapshot(captures.env|inventory|vault)` (`tools/companion/lib/simc-profile.js`), and nothing else in the addon writes gear to SavedVariables, so a refresh that only reloaded shipped whatever the owner had last captured **by hand**: the first real run, 2026-09-07, produced `spec=guardian` from the 09-05 capture, two days and one spec stale, with `region`/`level`/`race` missing because that snapshot predates S-2's reads. `Companion.Refresh()` now runs `env`, `inventory` and `vault` through `ns.RunCapture` in that order and only then calls `ReloadUI`; any refusal is repeated verbatim and stops the reload, so the owner never reloads believing a capture happened. **Why here and nowhere else:** not on `PLAYER_LOGOUT` (the client gives no reliable time there, and a capture at every logout writes SavedVariables nobody asked for); not in the companion (it cannot make the client read anything, only read what was written); not by having `Inventory`/`Vault` write snapshots on every scan (captures are the raw record the tests replay - one writer, or a golden stops meaning what it says). `journal` is excluded on purpose: it is asynchronous, the profile reads none of it, and a reload mid-walk would abandon it. The bank half of the chat line is read back out of the snapshot just taken rather than asked of the client again, so the words on screen are a fact about the bytes the companion will get. The cost is accepted and named: two refreshes now write six snapshots instead of none, and `/lootpath capture wipe` is still the only pruner.

- **2026-09-07 (C-2, WKE-534) - the companion file's shape, and who owns the game's copy of it.** Three things settled while building the addon half.
  (a) **The contract is one assignment and nothing else.** `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua` assigns `ns.companionVerdict = { writtenAt, companionVersion, exports = { {schema, contentType, json} } }`, Lua strings/numbers/tables only. The addon calls nothing in it, `loadstring`es nothing, and passes every field through `ns.Safe` before checking its type: it is the owner's own machine writing it, and it is still treated as data. `json` is QE Live's export text VERBATIM and goes through `ns.QEImport.Parse`, so the companion cannot smuggle past a pin the paste box enforces. The entry's `contentType` is advisory; the content type inside the JSON is what a verdict is filed under, because that is the one QE Live answered.
  (b) **The later of the file and the store wins, and the file is never re-imported.** A paste made after the companion wrote its file is kept, and said so in chat; a companion file newer than the stored import replaces it. Because the chunk loads at every login, the same `writtenAt` seen again is a no-op, not a re-import and not a chat line - otherwise every `/reload` would re-parse two exports and repeat itself. Where a verdict came from is stored on it (`source`), so the window and `/lootpath status` can say "pasted" or "companion, written N minute(s) ago" after a reload.
  (c) **`tools\sync.ps1` no longer owns the game's `Data\`** (this is §11's open question, now closed). A push copies every top-level child except `Data`, and writes the placeholder only when the game has no `QEVerdict.lua` at all - it must have one, because the `.toc` names it. `-IncludeData` overwrites it deliberately, for when the companion's file is what is broken. Measured against a scratch client tree, not the real one: the companion's file survived two plain pushes and was replaced by `-IncludeData`. The same measurement found that `Copy-Item -Path <dir> -Destination <that same dir>` NESTS (`Libs\Libs`) on a second push where `-Destination <its parent>` merges, which is why the loop names the parent.
  Rejected: gitignoring the placeholder (the `.toc` names it, so a release without it would not load); having the companion re-run after every sync (a sync is a dev action and the companion is not always up); a second `.toc` entry the companion creates (the file must exist for a fresh install). Upgrade Finder documents are refused by schema name until M3-6 lands, rather than handed to the Top Gear parser, which would report the wrong reason.

- **2026-09-04 - Addon over coach-workbench.** Bigger audience (every healer gears), Raidbots-shaped willingness to pay, and the only gap nobody fills. Workbench had better Healper reuse and a smaller market. Reuse does not drive bets.
- **2026-09-04 - Name: Lootpath.** Zero GitHub repos, no CurseForge hits (2026-09-04). CurseForge/Wago slugs to be claimed the day a public release is decided.
- **2026-09-05 - Licence: MIT.** Open source from day one; every dependency licence-checked.
- **2026-09-05 - Option A for the Upgrade Map.** Values-free for MVP: every spec-appropriate drop per slot by item level, QE deltas only where the Top Gear export covers the exact item (vault, owned). In parallel, prepare an Upgrade Finder export as a PR on the owner's fork (QE-1); owner offers it (QE-2); userscript scraper (QE-3) only if he declines - owner's call, recommendation is to leave the map values-free rather than scrape. Rejected: computing values ourselves (violates the product rule).
- **2026-09-05 - No own `/simc` exporter for MVP.** The SimulationCraft addon (Unlicense) already exports gear, bags and vault and is what QE Live expects. README tells the user to use it.
- **2026-09-05 - Tooltips descoped.** Pawn, GearingWise and GearWish already annotate tooltips; a fourth is not a gap. The dashboard and the loot map are the product.
- **2026-09-05 - Ace3: AceDB only.** UI is native frames + templates; options through the modern `Settings` API (`InterfaceOptions_AddCategory` removed in 10.0). No AceGUI.
- **2026-09-05 - rxi/json.lua, strict `version: 1` pin.** Any other version refused with a message naming both versions. No guessing at a moved schema.
- **2026-09-05 - Item identity = itemID + sorted bonus IDs.** Two copies of an item at different upgrade levels are different items to QE Live; an itemID-only match is never accepted silently.
- **2026-09-05 - Test strategy.** busted over pure modules with a hand-written WoW stub (`spec/stubs/wow.lua`) and **committed capture transcripts** from the owner's client (`/lootpath capture <name>` -> SavedVariables -> `sync.ps1 -Pull` -> `spec/fixtures/`). wowless (MIT, active) evaluated and rejected for now: no Encounter Journal stubs. Every guard proven red before it ships.
- **2026-09-05 - CI gates are real failures**: luacheck (LuaRocks, Lua 5.1, explicit WoW globals list), StyLua `--check`, LuaLS `--check` with Ketho's annotations (MIT), busted, packager zip on every PR. Branch protection requires all of them; agents merge their own PRs only when green; never bypass, never disable a test to get green.
- **2026-09-05 - Packager: BigWigsMods/packager@v2** (v2.5.1, 2025-12-02): zip artifact on PRs, GitHub release on `v*` tags. No CurseForge/Wago upload for the MVP.
- **2026-09-05 - Values-free note wording is pinned**: "Values shown are QE Live's, for items it has ranked. Other drops are listed by item level only."
- **2026-09-05 - Nothing runs in combat**; every client read passes `issecretvalue`/`issecrettable`; no network; no backend.
- **2026-09-05 - Captures `inventory` and `vault` ship in M0-1** (moved from M1-1 and M3-3) so M0-2's single game session yields three transcripts (env, inventory with bank open and closed, vault before and after opening the window). The journal capture stays in M3-1 because it needs the async walk. Captures only call functions named in `Captures.lua`; iterating a namespace and calling what it finds is forbidden (`C_Bank` moves items).
- **2026-09-05 - Local gates.** StyLua 2.5.2 and lua-language-server 3.18.2 via winget; luacheck and busted in Docker (`tools/docker/Dockerfile`, Alpine Lua 5.1 + LuaRocks) because Chocolatey needs an elevated shell the dev machine does not have. `tools/check.ps1` runs all four; CI is the authority.
- **2026-09-05 - Packager layout via `move-folders`.** `release.sh` requires `.git` in its topdir (measured in CI: `-t Lootpath` fails with "No Git, SVN, or Hg checkout found"), so it runs from the repo root with `package-as: Lootpath`, `move-folders: Lootpath/Lootpath: Lootpath`, externals at `Lootpath/Libs/...`, and `ignore` for `spec`, `tools`, `docs` and the root markdown. The zip holds `Lootpath/` with the toc, code, `Libs/` and the MIT `LICENSE` the packager copies in (verified locally with `release.sh -d -e -z`).
- **2026-09-06 - Inventory records speak QE Live's slot vocabulary** and carry only gear. WKE-516's example used `"HEAD"`; the export's `item.slot` values are the strings above, and Match (M2-2) groups by them, so one vocabulary is kept. Items with no gear slot (330 of 453 bag entries in the transcript were `INVTYPE_NON_EQUIP_IGNORE`) are skipped rather than carried as records with a nil slot.
- **2026-09-06 - `qe-live-upgradefinder` v1 schema (fork branch, not yet upstream).** `{ schema, version: 1, exportedAt, player {name, realm, region, spec, gameType}, contentType, reportId, settings (ufSettings), equipped[] (Top Gear item shape), items[] {id, level, slot, source {instanceId, encounterId}, dropLoc, dropType, dropDifficulty, upgradePercent, hpsGain, score} }`. **Positive `upgradePercent` / `hpsGain` means better.** Lootpath's future parser pins that sign in a test the way QEImport pins Top Gear's. Items carry `id` + `level`, not bonus IDs, because the site's stored report has none; the Upgrade Map joins them to the journal by itemID and item level.
- **2026-09-06 - Golden fixtures for normalisers.** The transcript is replayed through the stub (`spec/helpers/replay.lua`) and the module's output is committed under `spec/fixtures/expected/`, regenerated only by running busted with `LOOTPATH_WRITE_EXPECTED=1`. A golden guards regressions; the field-by-field tests against the raw returns are what prove correctness.
- **2026-09-06 - The sign convention has one definition in code**: `ns.QEImport.ALT_WORSE_SCORE_PERCENT_SIGN = 1`, `ALT_WORSE_HPS_DIFFERENCE_SIGN = -1`, and `ns.QEImport.AlternativeIsBetter(alt)`. M2-2 and M3-3 read the direction from that predicate and never from their own arithmetic, because getting the sign backwards is this module's whole failure mode. The spec pins the same two constants independently.
- **2026-09-06 - `version` must be the JSON number `1`.** A string `"1"` is refused rather than coerced: a changed type means the schema moved, and the pin exists to fail loudly rather than wrongly (extends the 2026-09-05 version-pin decision).
- **2026-09-06 - The verdict keeps `topSet.order` and a per-item `count`.** A map keyed by item key has no order and collapses a matched pair of identical rings into one entry; `order` (keys in the export's own order, duplicates repeated) and `count` keep both facts. QE Live's exporter has a commit named "handle duplicates", so duplicates are real.
- **2026-09-06 - Vault items are harvested from alternatives as well as the top set.** A vault option QE Live ranked *below* the top set appears only in a differential; excluding it would hide exactly the option the Vault panel must talk about.
- **2026-09-06 - `QEImport.Parse` carries no combat guard.** It is pure Lua over a pasted string; its one client read is `UnitName("player")` for the name-mismatch warning, and that passes `ns.Safe`. The combat rule binds the UI (M2-2) and the scanners, not a string parser.
- **2026-09-06 - Captures can be asynchronous.** `ns.RegisterCapture(name, help, run, { async = true })` hands `run` a `finish(data, failure)` callback and `RunCapture` answers `{ok=true, pending=true}`; the snapshot is stored when `finish` is called, and `finish` counts only once. The Encounter Journal loads loot only after `EJ_LOOT_DATA_RECIEVED`, so `capture journal` cannot be one synchronous call the way `env`, `inventory` and `vault` are. Two guards come with it: only one capture runs at a time, and one that never calls back is abandoned after `ns.CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (180) so the command cannot wedge. `RunCapture` also passes the rest of the slash command through as `args`, which is how `/lootpath capture journal 14` picks the M+ preview level.
- **2026-09-06 - `capture journal` registers in `Modules/Journal.lua`, not `Captures.lua`.** The rule is that a capture calls only functions its own file names, and the journal's list belongs beside the adapter that owns it; `Captures.lua`'s header points at it. `ns.captureOrder` is therefore `env, inventory, vault, journal`.
- **2026-09-06 - The journal capture is the one capture that is not purely a read**, and CLAUDE.md's rule is narrowed rather than broken. `EJ_SelectTier`, `EJ_SelectInstance`, `EJ_SetDifficulty`, `EJ_SetLootFilter` and `C_EncounterJournal.SetPreviewMythicPlusLevel` change the Adventure Guide's **view state** - what the journal is looking at - and nothing about the character, its items or its money. The walk reads the tier, difficulty and loot filter first and puts all three back when it finishes, **including when combat aborts it**. There is no getter for the M+ preview level, so that one cannot be restored: the owner is left with a preview level set in their Adventure Guide until they change it.
- **2026-09-06 - Difficulty IDs come from the client's own `DifficultyUtil.ID`,** with the values read from Blizzard's shipped `DifficultyUtil.lua` as the fallback (`Adapter.DIFFICULTY`). The journal's Mythic+ difficulty is `DungeonChallenge` (8), which is in Blizzard's own `EJ_DIFFICULTIES` list - not a wiki claim.
- **2026-09-06 - Both map-to-journal-instance lookups are recorded side by side, and a map neither resolves is recorded rather than dropped.** `capture journal` runs `C_EncounterJournal.GetInstanceForGameMap(mapID)` and the global `EJ_GetInstanceForMap(mapID)` against the same `GetMapUIInfo().mapID` for every map in `C_ChallengeMode.GetMapTable()`, and `pool.maps[i].resolvedBy` names whichever answered. This is the step §11 calls the most likely to differ from any doc, so the transcript decides it instead of the code assuming it.
- **2026-09-06 - An adapter's list of client functions is names, not values.** A table entry whose value is `nil` is simply absent, so a table of values cannot tell "this client does not have that function" from "not listed" - which is exactly what the capture has to record. `Adapter.FUNCTION_NAMES` is a list of strings and `Adapter.Resolve` looks one up; every real call site still names its function literally, so nothing is ever called because a lookup found it. Found by the test that deletes a function and expects `ok = false`.
- **2026-09-06 - The journal walk reads every target twice, mirroring `EncounterJournal_LootUpdate`.** Blizzard's `EncounterJournal_OnEvent` splits `EJ_LOOT_DATA_RECIEVED` two ways: an event carrying an itemID while the list is current refreshes just that row (`EncounterJournal_LootCallback`), anything else re-reads the whole list (`EncounterJournal_LootUpdate`, an `EJ_GetNumLoot` loop over `GetLootInfoByIndex`). The walk mirrors the second, because it has no frame per row to refresh and cannot come back to a target once the next `EJ_SelectInstance` has moved the journal on. `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` was the alternative and was **not** taken: Blizzard's own journal never calls it in the loot path, 351 events fired unprompted in the 09-06 walk, and adding a client function nothing has been observed to need is a guess. Both reads are kept in the capture so Tuesday's `capture journal` shows what the second one filled.
- **2026-09-06 - A link-less loot row is `pending`, not "no item level".** 240 of the 376 itemIDs in the 09-06 walk were link-less on *every* row they appeared in; treating a row with no equipLoc as non-gear would silently lose 64% of the map. So a pending row is carried with a nil `itemLevel` and `slot` and `pending = true`, and a row that is known and known not to be gear (a pattern, a mount, a toy - 55 of 613) is dropped the way Inventory drops one. The consequence is deliberate: an item that is a mount in one target and link-less in another stays in the map through the link-less row, because a link-less row cannot be told from gear.
- **2026-09-06 - `Journal` and `Inventory` share one slot table.** The journal's own `EncounterJournalItemInfo.slot` and the row's `GetItemInfoInstant` equipLoc agree one-for-one across all 244 rows of the 09-06 walk that carried both (`One-Hand`/`INVTYPE_WEAPON`, `Held In Off-hand`/`INVTYPE_HOLDABLE`), but the first is the client's localised UI text and the second is not. `ns.Journal` therefore goes through `ns.Inventory.SlotForEquipLoc`, which is what makes M3-3's join between the map and the scanner a join on one vocabulary - QE Live's (extends the 2026-09-06 Inventory decision).
- **2026-09-06 - A boss name is an instance-wide fact.** `EJ_GetEncounterInfoByIndex` answered nothing on every `DungeonChallenge` target and on both Midnight targets in the 09-06 walk, leaving 95 of 613 rows with no boss name in their own target. The aggregator indexes names per instance across every difficulty, which names 73 of those 95; the walk now also calls `EJ_GetEncounterInfo(encounterID)` per row, exactly as Blizzard's loot button does, which the next capture proves for the remaining 22.
- **2026-09-06 - The journal cache is keyed on the build NUMBER**, `GetBuildInfo()`'s second return (69587), not the version string: a hotfix build can move item levels without moving "12.1.0". Every entry from another build is dropped whenever `Build` runs, so a stale map cannot outlive a patch.
- **2026-09-06 - QE Live's content types are `"Raid"` and `"Dungeon"`, and "Mythic+" is not one of them.** `src/globalTypes.d.ts` line 130 declares `contentTypes = "Raid" | "Dungeon"`, `Redux/Reducers/RootReducer.ts` defaults the store to `"Raid"`, `SetupAndMenus/Header/ContentToggle.js` offers exactly those two toggle values, and the exporter writes `result.contentType || ""`. `Dungeon` IS the Mythic+ side. `ns.DB_DEFAULTS.profile.settings.contentType` therefore moved from `"Mythic+"` to `"Dungeon"`: the old default could never have matched an export's `contentType`, so the M2-2 setting would have silently done nothing. The two strings live once, in `ns.QEImport.CONTENT_TYPES`, and are compared to `verdict.contentType` with no translation between.
- **2026-09-06 - one import is kept per content type, as well as the most recent one.** `db.char.qeImport` still holds the last paste (M2-1's contract, unchanged), and `QEImport.Store` also files the verdict under `db.char.qeImports[contentType]`. Without that, the setting "which content type's verdict to show if the user pasted both" has nothing to choose between - the second paste would have overwritten the first. An export with no `contentType` files under `"Unknown"` rather than being dropped or being made to pretend it is one of QE Live's two.
- **2026-09-06 - Match makes one row per item in `topSet.order`, not one per slot name.** The export lists Finger twice and Trinket twice; a row per slot name would collapse a matched pair into one row and lose half the answer. Rows sort by a canonical slot order (read off the real export's own item order) with the export's sequence as the tiebreak, so the panel reads like a character sheet.
- **2026-09-06 - `dstSlot` comes from the scan, never from a table of slot IDs.** The equipment slot Lootpath equips into is the `slotIndex` the Inventory scan actually found the replaced item in (`INVSLOT_FIRST_EQUIPPED..LAST`, measured 1..19 on this client), handed out per slot in slot order. Hardcoding `INVSLOT_FINGER1 = 11` would have been a remembered number; this is a measured one. When the slot is empty there is no number to use and `dstSlot` stays nil, which is the client's own "you choose" case.
- **2026-09-06 - an itemID-only match is refused, and the itemID + item-level fallback is loud.** Extends the 2026-09-05 identity decision with the mechanism: `matchedBy` on the row, an entry in `result.fallbacks`, and a line in the chat frame naming both sets of bonus IDs. Proven red by making the fallback silent and by widening it to itemID only.
- **2026-09-06 - the panel keeps the last scan on screen in combat rather than blanking.** `Inventory.Scan` refuses in combat, so a naive refresh at `PLAYER_REGEN_DISABLED` would empty the window exactly when the user has stopped being able to fix it. The last good match stays, marked `stale`, the summary says so, and every button is disabled - and `EquipPanel.Equip` re-checks `InCombatLockdown()` itself, so a lockdown arriving between the draw and the click still refuses.
- **2026-09-06 - the export's age is computed without assuming a timezone.** `exportedAt` is ISO 8601 in UTC (`2026-09-06T21:14:24.465Z`). `time(fields)` reads its fields as LOCAL time and `date("!*t", t)` writes UTC ones, so `now - time(date("!*t", now))` is this machine's offset from UTC and adding it back turns the UTC fields into an epoch. The spec installs fake clocks at UTC, -5h, +9.5h and +14h and demands the same answer from all four; with the offset forced to zero, that test is the one that goes red.
- **2026-09-06 - the WoW stub grew a widget model.** Frames, regions, buttons, an editbox, GameTooltip and the Settings API, modelling the API's CONTRACT and nothing about pixels: `SetMaxLetters(0)` means no limit and any other value truncates; a disabled button swallows `Click` but still fires `OnEnter`. That is what makes "the editbox handler calls Parse" and "a 200 KB paste is not truncated" testable at all. It proves nothing about how the window looks or behaves on the owner's screen, which stays an in-game step (M2-3).
- **2026-09-06 - The journal source entry carries `itemKey` and `name`, and M3-3's join is the exact key and nothing looser.** WKE-524 asks the Upgrade Map to show a QE delta "where a candidate appears in the QE verdict (by item key)", and the map as M3-1 shipped it had no key at all, so the key was added: `ns.ItemKey` over the loot row's own link, nil on a pending row for the same reason `itemLevel` is. **Measured over the 2026-09-06 walk: all 189 keyed entries carry exactly ONE bonus ID, and it is 3524 on every one of them**, while the 15 items of the committed Top Gear export carry two to seven. Exactly one itemID is in both (251153, Arctic Explorer's Legwraps, Feet, Sentinel of Winter in Den of Nalorakk, 276 Heroic / 292 Mythic / 305 at M+10) and even there the keys differ. **So the Upgrade Map is values-free in practice as well as in principle: 0 of 558 sources carry a number.** The looser joins were considered and rejected - matching on itemID would put a number about the copy you own (263-321 with its own bonus IDs) next to a preview of a different item level, which is the estimate this addon never makes. The rule stands as written and the panel simply shows nothing; when the fork's Upgrade Finder export lands (QE-1/QE-2) it joins by itemID + item level as the 2026-09-06 schema decision says, and that is a different, honest join.
- **2026-09-06 - A vault reward's `type` and `itemDBID` are Blizzard's documented shape, not a measured one.** All four committed `capture vault` snapshots show `rewards` empty on every activity except Concession, which held a currency (`{ id = 3513, type = 2, quantity = 1 }`, `Enum.CachedRewardType.Currency`) and no `itemDBID`. `Modules/Vault.lua` is therefore written to `WeeklyRewardActivityRewardInfo` (`{ type, id, quantity, itemDBID? }`) from Blizzard's exported docs under `.luals/`, its tests drive that shape through the stub and say so in the file, and WKE-523's after-reset capture is what turns it from documented into measured. The alternative - guessing from the wiki - is the thing this project does not do.
- **2026-09-06 - Guard the value, then coerce it.** `tonumber(secret)` is `nil`, so `guarded(counter, tonumber(x))` silently swallows a secret and leaves the count at zero. Every read in `Modules/Vault.lua` passes `ns.Safe` first and is coerced after. Found by the test that made an activity's `threshold` secret and expected `secretsSeen` to be 1.
- **2026-09-06 - `spec/helpers/replay.lua` caches one transcript table, so a test that mutates it poisons every test after it.** `spec/journal_spec.lua`'s "prefers the second read" spliced a one-row second read into target 1 of the shared snapshot; every `it` declared after it therefore saw 588 rows instead of 613, and **the committed golden `spec/fixtures/expected/journal-20260906-161213.lua` was generated from that corrupted state** (533 sources, 375 items reported as 357, `rereadTargets = 1`). It passed because the write and the compare were equally wrong. M3-3 made that test work on `Stub.deepcopy(snapshot)` and regenerated the golden, which now reads 613 rows -> 558 sources over 375 items, matching what §0 and §11 have said all along. A test that mutates a fixture copies it first.
- **2026-09-06 - `QEImport.Coverage(verdict, key)` is the one lookup both M3-3 panels use**, so "covered" means the same thing on the Upgrade Map and in the Vault. A top-set item comes back with **no numbers** - the export gives per-item deltas only on its differentials, and inventing one for a top-set item would be Lootpath computing a healer value - so the panels say "QE Live: in your best set" there. An item in several alternatives is reported through the best of them by `QEImport.AlternativeRank`, which is the pinned sign constant and not fresh arithmetic.
- **2026-09-06 - Staleness is measured against the client's own reset boundary, never against a calendar.** `VaultPanel.IsVerdictStale` computes `nextReset = now + C_DateAndTime.GetSecondsUntilWeeklyReset()` and calls a verdict stale when its `exportedAt` predates `nextReset - 7 days`. `EpochFromISO` reads QE Live's UTC stamp through the client's own clock offset (`time() - time(date("!*t"))`), and answers nil for a stamp it cannot read, so an unreadable stamp produces no claim rather than a wrong one.
- **2026-09-06 - The Upgrade Map reads the newest stored journal walk, and says so when there is none.** `capture journal` is the only thing that produces a walk today (M3-1), so `Panel.Gather` reads `db.global.captures.journal`, and an empty map renders "No loot map yet. Run /lootpath capture journal out of combat to walk the Adventure Guide." rather than an empty season. **Driving a walk from a button in the window is still not built**: the tab exists now, but the walk changes the Adventure Guide's view state and takes seconds, so putting it behind a button is its own decision, and M3-4 is what says whether it is needed.
- **2026-09-06 - The window has one tab per promise, and only the visible tab is drawn.** M3-3's panels hang off the frame M2-2 built rather than opening windows of their own: `UI.TABS` names the three, `UI.ShowTab` owns which is visible, and `UI.Refresh` redraws that one alone. Redrawing all three on every `PLAYER_EQUIPMENT_CHANGED` and `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` would rebuild the journal map - 613 rows - to update a panel nobody is looking at. Blizzard's `PanelTemplates_SetTab`/`SetNumTabs` are called only for the selected/deselected artwork, through a `type(...) == "function"` check, because the tab has to keep working on a client that does not have them; what the tabs LOOK like is M3-4, in game.
- **2026-09-06 - A panel reads `ns.UI.ActiveVerdict()`, never `QEImport.Current()`.** M2-2 files one import per content type and the setting chooses between them, saying out loud when it falls back. A panel that reached for the most recent paste instead would show a Raid answer under a Dungeon setting with nothing said - which is the failure `ActiveVerdict` was written to prevent. Both M3-3 panels go through it and fall back to `QEImport.Current()` only when there is no window around them (a panel built directly in a test). Proven red on both panels by a character carrying a Dungeon and a Raid import at once.
- **2026-09-07 - A difficulty label is unique across a map, and the whole map is what decides it.** `GetDifficultyInfo` is the client's own localised name and stays the first choice, but it answers the bare word for dungeon Heroic (2) and raid Heroic (15) alike and for dungeon Mythic (23) and raid Mythic (16) alike - measured in game 2026-09-06, where the filter row read "Heroic (67)", "Mythic Keystone 10 (54)", "Heroic (38)", "Mythic (39)" and a clipped fifth. `Panel.DifficultyLabels` therefore labels every difficulty in a map together: a name nothing else shares keeps the client's localised word, and every member of a colliding group falls back to `Panel.DIFFICULTY_NAME`'s already-qualified form ("Heroic dungeon", "Heroic raid"), with the ID appended if even that collides. The set is the whole map's difficulties, never the filtered subset, so clicking a filter cannot rename the buttons. The alternative - qualifying always - was rejected because it would replace a localised name with an English one on every client, in the common case where nothing is ambiguous.
- **2026-09-07 - The filter row wraps, and the wrap is a pure function.** Five buttons at a fixed 120 points ran off the 620-wide window in game (2026-09-06, the fifth clipped). `Panel.FilterLayout` packs labels greedily into rows of a given width and returns `{ rows, buttons = { { index, row, column, width } } }`, so the wrap is a headless assertion rather than something only the owner's eye can check; `Panel.Refresh` re-anchors on every draw (the labels, and so the widths, change with the map) and moves the scroll frame down by however many rows it took. Words are kept - shortening "Heroic dungeon (67)" to fit would trade a clipped button for an unreadable one. The width comes from the button's own font string in the client and from a per-character estimate headlessly, which is what makes the layout test deterministic.
- **2026-09-07 - The pinned note belongs to the panel header, not to the list.** Both M3-3 panels put `note = Panel.NOTE` in the model AND printed it as the list's first line, while `Panel.Create` had already drawn it above the list - so it appeared twice on the Upgrade Map and the Vault tab in game (2026-09-06). `Panel.Lines` no longer emits it on either panel; the model keeps `note` because that is what pins the wording headlessly, and the wording itself is unchanged (2026-09-05).
- **2026-09-07 - A drop the client reports at item level 1 is hidden, and the slot says how many.** `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` answers `{1, false, 1}` for the cosmetic and quest drops the Adventure Guide lists beside real loot; they sort to the bottom of every slot. The owner's decision is to hide them behind `Panel.LEVEL_ONE_NOTE` ("N drops at item level 1 hidden: cosmetic and quest items"). This is not Lootpath forming a view about an item: 1 is the CLIENT's own figure, so hiding on it is a filter on a fact, and the count line is what keeps the list honest. Exactly 1 and no other threshold - the 12 rows at level 44 in the same walk are real loot and are shown. A pending row is never hidden, because its level is unknown rather than 1 (the same distinction `PENDING_NOTE` exists for), and the aggregator still carries every row: `counts.hiddenLevelOne` is the panel's own bookkeeping, and `ns.Journal` is untouched.
- **2026-09-07 - An unsubstituted packager token is not a version.** The `.toc` ships `## Version: @project-version@` and the BigWigs packager substitutes it only when it builds a release, so a copy `tools\sync.ps1` puts in the client carries the token itself and the title bar read "Lootpath @project-version@" (measured in game 2026-09-06). `ns.VERSION` treats a metadata value starting with `@` as absent and falls back to the `"dev"` that was already on that line. Fixed in Lua rather than in `sync.ps1` or the `.toc`, which stay exactly what the packager reads.
- **2026-09-07 - "Concession" stays the label for `WeeklyRewardChestThresholdType` 5, and it was checked.** Blizzard's own `Blizzard_WeeklyRewards.lua` (under `.luals/`) titles each vault row through `SetUpActivity(frame, LABEL, atlas, type)` with the global strings `RAIDS`, `DUNGEONS`, `PVP` and `WORLD` - and Concession is not one of them: it is a separate `ConcessionFrame` on `WeeklyRewardsConcessionMixin`, with the threshold type as an XML KeyValue, no category title at all, and only `WEEKLY_REWARDS_CONCESSION_FORMAT` (an icon and a quantity) as text. There is no display string to borrow, so the enum's own name stands.
